\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\big(\{|M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}}-t|\leq\varepsilon\}\cap\mathcal{E}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\{|M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}}-t|\leq\varepsilon\}\cap\mathcal{E}^{c}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})+\mathbb{P}\big(\big|\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}X_{j}-\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash{\mathcal{N}}_{\delta}}X_{i^{\prime}}-t\big|\leq\varepsilon\big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\omega_{\delta}+7\left\{\!\mathbb{E}\!\left(\!\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}|X_{i}-\mu_{i}|\right)\wedge\mathbb{E}\!\left(\!\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}|X_{j}-\mu_{j}|\right)\right\}\!\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta\sigma^{2}},

where the first inequality holds by definitions and
\operatorname{Corr}(X_{i},X_{j})<1-\delta
for any
i\in\mathcal{A}\backslash{\mathcal{N}}_{\delta}
and
j\in\mathcal{B}
,
the second inequality holds by Theorem
2.1
. Then (
5.2
) follows, and (
2.4
) holds by taking the infimum on
\delta
that satisfies
{\mathcal{N}}_{\delta}\neq\mathcal{A}
, which concludes our proof.

5.3
Proof of Proposition
2.3

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\mu_{0}=0
.
We begin with showing the first inequality of (
2.6
). Recall that by definition,
\mathcal{L}(M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon)=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}(|M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}}-t|\leq\varepsilon)
. Define
{\mathcal{N}}=\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{B}=[m]\backslash[m-k]
, and the events

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\left\{\max_{i\in{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}\geq\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}\backslash{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}\right\},\quad\mathcal{E}_{2}=\left\{\max_{j\in{\mathcal{N}}}X_{j}\geq\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}\backslash{\mathcal{N}}}X_{j}\right\},\quad\text{and }\quad\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2},

where we note that
\mathcal{E}=\left\{\max_{i\in{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}\geq\max_{i\in[p]\backslash{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}\right\}
. Taking
t=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}}|\leq\varepsilon\big)\geq\mathbb{P}\big(\big\{|M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}}|\leq\varepsilon\big\}\cap\mathcal{E}\big)

(5.4)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\big(\{|\textstyle\max_{i\in{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}-\textstyle\max_{i\in{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}|\leq\varepsilon\}\cap\mathcal{E}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\big(\{0\leq\varepsilon\}\cap\mathcal{E}\big)=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),

where the first equality holds because under the event
\mathcal{E}
, we have that
M_{\mathcal{B}}=M_{\mathcal{A}}=\max_{i\in{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}
.
Note that by the exchangeability of
(X_{1},\ldots,X_{p})^{\top}
in (
2.5
) and the assumption that
\bar{\rho}:=\max_{i,j\in[p],i\neq j}\operatorname{Corr}(X_{i},X_{j})<1
, for any subset
{\mathcal{S}}\subseteq[p]
with
|{\mathcal{S}}|\geq 2
, we have that the maximizer of
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}}
is unique with probability 1, and for any
j\in{\mathcal{S}}
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\textstyle\arg\max_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}}X_{i}=j)=1/|{\mathcal{S}}|.

(5.5)

Therefore, taking
{\mathcal{S}}=[p]
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})=\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathop{\text{\rm arg\,max}}_{i\in[p]}X_{i}\in{\mathcal{N}}\Big)=\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{N}}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathop{\text{\rm arg\,max}}_{i\in[p]}X_{i}=j\Big)=\frac{k}{p},

(5.6)

which combined with (
5.4
) gives the first inequality of (
2.6
).

Next, we prove the second inequality of (
2.6
). Following similar arguments to (
5.6
), by taking
{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{A}
and
{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{B}
in (
5.5
), respectively, we have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1})=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2})=k/m=2k/(p+k)
.
Then, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
\varepsilon>0
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}}-t|\leq\varepsilon\big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\!\big\{|M_{\mathcal{B}}\!-\!M_{\mathcal{A}}\!-\!t|\leq\varepsilon\big\}\!\cap\!(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{c}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}^{c})\big)+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\Big|\max_{i\in\mathcal{B}\backslash{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}\backslash{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}-t\Big|\leq\varepsilon\right)+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\leq 7\left\{\mathbb{E}\!\Big[\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}\backslash{\mathcal{N}}}\!|X_{i}-\mu_{0}|\!\Big]\wedge\mathbb{E}\!\Big[\max_{i\in\mathcal{B}\backslash{\mathcal{N}}}\!|X_{i}-\mu_{0}|\!\Big]\right\}\frac{\varepsilon}{(1-\bar{\rho})\sigma^{2}}+\frac{2k}{m},

where the second inequality holds due to the fact that under the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{c}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}^{c}
, we have
M_{\mathcal{B}}=\max_{i\in\mathcal{B}\backslash{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}
and
M_{\mathcal{A}}=\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}\backslash{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}
, and the last inequality holds by Theorem
2.1
and plugging in
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1})=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2})=k/m
.

Finally, we prove (
2.7
). For any
t\in\mathbb{R}
such that
|t|>\varepsilon
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}}-t|\leq\varepsilon\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\!\big\{|M_{\mathcal{B}}\!-\!M_{\mathcal{A}}\!-\!t|\!\leq\!\varepsilon\big\}\!\cap\!\mathcal{E}\big)\!+\!\mathbb{P}\big(\!\big\{|M_{\mathcal{B}}\!-\!M_{\mathcal{A}}\!-\!t|\!\leq\!\varepsilon\big\}\!\cap\!\mathcal{E}_{1}^{c}\big)\!

\displaystyle\quad+\!\mathbb{P}\big(\!\big\{|M_{\mathcal{B}}\!-\!M_{\mathcal{A}}\!-\!t|\!\leq\!\varepsilon\big\}\!\cap\!\mathcal{E}_{2}^{c}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\Big|\max_{i\in\mathcal{B}}X_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}\backslash{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}-t\Big|\leq\varepsilon\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\Big|\max_{i\in\mathcal{B}\backslash{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}X_{i}-t\Big|\leq\varepsilon\right)+\mathbb{P}(|t|\leq\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq 14\left\{\mathbb{E}\!\Big[\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\!|X_{i}-\mu|\!\Big]\wedge\mathbb{E}\!\Big[\max_{i\in\mathcal{B}}\!|X_{i}-\mu|\!\Big]\right\}\frac{\varepsilon}{(1-\bar{\rho})\sigma^{2}},

where the second inequality holds by the fact that under the event
\mathcal{E}
, we have

\big\{|M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}}-t|\leq\varepsilon\big\}=\big\{|\max_{i\in{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}-\max_{i\in{\mathcal{N}}}X_{i}-t|\leq\varepsilon\big\}=\big\{|t|\leq\varepsilon\big\},

and the last one holds by Theorem
2.1
and the assumption
|t|>\varepsilon
. Thus, (
2.7
) follows.

5.4
Proofs of results in Section
3

We provide the proof of
Theorem
3.1
in Section
3
. Before we delve into the proof, we provide some auxiliary lemmas to be used in the proof.

We first provide a comparison inequality using the Stein kernel or the difference between the maxima of two random vectors, which is a modified version of Theorem 3.2 in
[
12
]
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{b}^{2}(\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be the class of twice continuously differentiable functions, with both the functions and their first and second-order partial derivatives bounded. Let
V\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be a centered random vector, and suppose that there exists a mensurable function (Stein kernel)
\tau:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
such that

\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}(\partial_{j}\varphi(V)V_{j})=\sum_{j,k=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}(\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi(V)\tau_{jk}(V)),\quad\forall\varphi\in\mathcal{C}_{b}^{2}(\cdot).

(5.7)

Let
Z\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be a centered Gaussian random vector with covariance matrix
\Sigma
, and let
\{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\}
be a nontrivial partition of
[p]
. Suppose that
Z
satisfies Condition
3.1
for the partition
\{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\}
. Then, we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R},v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left|\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(V)-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(V)\leq s\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(Z)-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(Z)\leq s\Big)\right|

(5.8)

\displaystyle\leq K\cdot\frac{\mathbb{E}(\max_{\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\rm p}}|Z_{\ell}|/\sigma_{\ell})}{C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}}\sqrt{\Delta\log p},

where
\mathcal{M}_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{v}(x)=\max_{j\in{\mathcal{S}}}(x_{j}+v_{j})
for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
{\mathcal{S}}\in\{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\}
,
K>0
is a universal constant, and
\Delta=\mathbb{E}(\max_{j,k\in[p]}|\tau_{jk}(V)-\Sigma_{jk}|)
.

Proof.

See Appendix
A.4
.
∎

Next, we provide a tailored version of Theorem 3.1 in
[
12
]
. We modify the statistics and anti-concentration conditions in
[
12
]
to our setting.
Let
V_{1},\ldots,V_{n},Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be independent zero-mean random vectors, and
B_{n}\geq 1
is the rate defined in (
3.4
) and (
3.5
). We impose the following conditions on the random vectors.

Condition 5.1
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
j\in[p]
, we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[V_{ij}^{4}+Z_{ij}^{4}]\leq CB_{n}^{2}.

Condition 5.2
.

There exists a constant
C\geq 1
such that for all
i\in[n]
, we have

\mathbb{P}\big(\|V_{i}\|_{\infty}\vee\|Z_{i}\|_{\infty}>CB_{n}\log(pn)\big)\leq 1/n^{4}.

Condition 5.3
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
i\in[n]
, we have

\mathbb{E}\big[\|V_{i}\|_{\infty}^{8}+\|Z_{i}\|_{\infty}^{8}\big]\leq CB_{n}^{8}\log^{8}(pn).

Condition 5.4
.

Define
S_{n}^{Z}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}
and let
\{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\}
be the non-trivial partition of
[p]
specified in Theorem
3.1
. There exists a constant
C>0
, and deterministic sequences
\varepsilon_{n}\geq 0
and
r_{n}\geq 1
such that for
any
t\in(0,\infty)
, we have

\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},s\in\mathbb{R}}\!\Big\{\!\mathbb{P}\Big(\!\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})\leq s+t\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})\leq s\Big)\!\!\Big\}\leq C(r_{n}t+\varepsilon_{n}),

where
\mathcal{M}_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{v}(x):=\max_{j\in{\mathcal{S}}}(x_{j}+v_{j})
for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, and
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
stands for an arbitrary shift in the mean of the random vector.

Conditions
5.1
-
5.3
correspond to the Conditions V, P and B given in Section 3.1 of
[
12
]
, and Condition
5.4
is the anti-concentration condition for
S_{n}^{Z}
corresponding to Condition A in
[
12
]
, which is tailored to fit our setting.
Then we present a modification of Theorem 3.1 in
[
12
]
(see Appendix
A.5
for the proof).

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
V_{1},\ldots,V_{n},Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be independent centered random vectors, and suppose that Conditions
5.1
-
5.4
hold. Assume that

\max_{j,k\in[p]}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathbb{E}(V_{ij}V_{ik})-\mathbb{E}(Z_{ij}Z_{ik})\big)\right|\leq C_{0}B_{n}\sqrt{\log(pn)},

(5.9)

and

\max_{j,k,\ell\in[p]}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathbb{E}(V_{ij}V_{ik}V_{i\ell})-\mathbb{E}(Z_{ij}Z_{ik}Z_{i\ell})\big)\right|\leq C_{0}B_{n}^{2}\sqrt{\log^{3}(pn)},

(5.10)

for some constant
C_{0}>0
. Then letting
S_{n}^{V}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}
and
S_{n}^{Z}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}
, under the condition that
B_{n}^{2}\log^{5}(pn)\leq c_{0}n
for some small enough constant
c_{0}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},s\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(S_{n}^{V})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(S_{n}^{V})\leq s\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})\leq s\Big)\right|

(5.11)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\left(r_{n}\left(\frac{B_{n}^{2}\log^{3}(pn)}{n}\right)^{1/4}+\varepsilon_{n}\right),

where
C_{1}>0
is a constant only depending on
c_{0},C_{0}
and the constants in Conditions
5.1
-
5.4
.

We then prove Theorem
3.1
.

Proof of Theorem
3.1
.

Recall that due to the constructions of
X
,
Y
and
\widehat{X}
, without loss of generality, we can assume
\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i})=\mu=\mathbf{0}
for
i\in[n]
. Similarly to the proof of Theorem 2.1 in
[
12
]
, we denote by
\mathcal{A}_{n}
the event that (33) and (35)-(37) in
[
12
]
hold, i.e.,
\mathcal{A}_{n}=\mathcal{A}_{n,1}\cap\mathcal{A}_{n,2}\cap\mathcal{A}_{n,3}\cap\mathcal{A}_{n,4}
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{n,1}

\displaystyle=\big\{\textstyle\max_{i\in[n]}\|\xi_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 5B_{n}\log(pn)\big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{n,2}

\displaystyle=\big\{\textstyle\max_{j\in[p]}\frac{1}{n}\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\xi_{ij}-\bar{\xi}_{j})^{4}\leq 2B_{n}^{2}b_{0}^{2}\big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{n,3}

\displaystyle=\big\{\textstyle\max_{j,k\in[p]}\big|n^{-1/2}\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}[(\xi_{ij}-\bar{\xi}_{j})(\xi_{ik}-\bar{\xi}_{k})-(\Sigma_{i})_{jk}]\big|\leq CB_{n}\sqrt{\log(pn)}\big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{n,4}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\!\max_{j,k,\ell\in[p]}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[(\xi_{ij}-\bar{\xi}_{j})(\xi_{ik}-\bar{\xi}_{k})(\xi_{i\ell}-\bar{\xi}_{\ell})-\mathbb{E}[\xi_{ij}\xi_{ik}\xi_{i\ell}]]\Big|\!\leq\!CB_{n}^{2}\sqrt{\log^{3}(pn)}\Big\},

where
C>0
is a universal constant,
B_{n}\geq 1
and
b_{0}>0
are defined in (
3.4
) and (
3.5
), and
\bar{\xi}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}
. Also, we let

\nu_{n,1}=\frac{\mathbb{E}[\max_{\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\rm p}}|X_{\ell}-\mathbb{E}(X_{\ell})|/\sigma_{\ell}]}{C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}}\left(\frac{B_{n}^{2}\log^{3}(pn)}{n}\right)^{1/4}\!\!\!,\quad\nu_{n,2}=\sqrt{\frac{B_{n}^{2}\log^{3}(pn)}{n}},

(5.12)

and, without loss of generality, we assume
1/(2n^{4})+1/n+3\nu_{n,2}<1
by taking the constant
C>0
in (
3.6
) large enough. Then by Lemma 4.1 and Lemma 4.2 in
[
12
]
, we have that

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{n})\geq 1-1/(2n^{4})-1/n-3\nu_{n,2}>0.

(5.13)

Then we will show that (
3.6
) and (
3.7
) hold conditioning on the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
via triangle inequality. Specifically, as in
[
12
]
, we define the multiplier bootstrap random vector generated from independent standardized
{\rm Beta}(1/2,3/2)
random variables,

X^{*}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\kappa_{i}(\xi_{i}-\bar{\xi})+a],

(5.14)

where
\kappa_{i}=\operatorname{Var}(\kappa_{i}^{0})^{-1/2}(\kappa_{i}^{0}-\mathbb{E}(\kappa_{i}^{0}))
and
\kappa_{i}^{0}\overset{\rm i.i.d.}{\sim}{\rm Beta}(1/2,3/2)
are independent of
\xi_{1:n}=\{\xi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. We propose the following claims, based on which we establish (
3.6
) and (
3.7
) via the triangle inequality:

Claim 5.1
.

Under the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, the following holds,

\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},s\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({X^{*}})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({X^{*}})\!\leq s\,|\,\xi_{1:n})-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({X})\!-\!\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({X})\leq s)\right|\lesssim\nu_{n,1}.

(5.15)

Proof.

See Section
5.4.1
.
∎

Claim 5.2
.

Under the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, we have

\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},s\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({X^{*}})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({X^{*}})\leq s\,|\,\xi_{1:n})-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({Y})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({Y})\leq s)\right|\lesssim\nu_{n,1}.

(5.16)

Proof.

See Section
5.4.2
.
∎

Claim 5.3
.

Under the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, we have

\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},s\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({X})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({X})\leq s)-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({\widehat{X}})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(\widehat{X})\leq s\,|\,\xi_{1:n})\right|\lesssim\nu_{n,1}.

(5.17)

Proof.

See Section
5.4.3
.
∎

By the triangle inequality, combining (
5.15
) and (
5.16
) shows that (
3.6
) holds under the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
. Furthermore, since both sides of (
3.6
) are deterministic and
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{n})>0
, we have that (
3.6
) holds unconditionally. Combining (
5.15
), (
5.15
) and (
5.17
), we have that (
3.7
) holds under the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
by triangle inequality. Besides, by (
5.13
), we have that
\mathcal{A}_{n}
holds with probability at least
1-1/(2n^{4})-1/n-3({B_{n}^{2}\log^{3}(pn)/n})^{1/2}
, and the results of Theorem
3.1
hold subsequently.
∎

5.4.1
Proof of Claim
5.1

We will apply Lemma
5.1
to show (
5.15
). Specifically, let
\tau(\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
be the Stein kernel of
X^{*}
defined in (
5.7
) conditioned on
\xi_{1:n}
. We will check the rate of
\Delta=\mathbb{E}(\max_{j,k\in[p]}|\tau_{jk}(V)-\Sigma_{jk}|)
.
By Lemma 4.6 of
[
26
]
,
under event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, we have that

\mathbb{E}\Big(\max_{j,k\in[p]}\big|\tau_{jk}(X^{*})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\xi_{ij}-\bar{\xi}_{j})(\xi_{ik}-\bar{\xi}_{k})\big|\,\,\,\Big|\xi_{1:n}\Big)\leq C_{1}B_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}

(5.18)

where
C_{1}>0
is a constant. Further, under the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\max_{j,k\in[p]}\left|\tau_{jk}(X^{*})-\Sigma_{jk}^{*}\right|\,\,\Big|\xi_{1:n}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \leq\mathbb{E}\Big(\max_{j,k\in[p]}\big|\tau_{jk}(X^{*})\!-\!\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\!\xi_{ij}-\bar{\xi}_{j}\!)(\!\xi_{ik}-\bar{\xi}_{k})\big|\Big|\xi_{1:n}\Big)\!+\!\max_{j,k\in[p]}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\!\xi_{ij}-\bar{\xi}_{j})(\!\xi_{ik}\!-\!\bar{\xi}_{k})-\Sigma_{jk}^{*}\Big|

\displaystyle\ \leq C_{1}B_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}+C_{2}B_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log(pn)}{n}}

\displaystyle\ \leq C_{3}B_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log(pn)}{n}},

where
\Sigma^{*}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Sigma_{i}
with
\Sigma_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i}\xi_{i}^{\top}]
. The first inequality holds by the triangle inequality, the second inequality holds by (
5.18
) together with that event
\mathcal{A}_{n,3}
holds,
and the last inequality holds for a large enough
C_{3}
.
Also, by assumption, the random vector
X
satisfies the covariance Condition
3.1
. Applying Lemma
5.1
, under the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, we have that
\Delta=C_{3}B_{n}\sqrt{{\log(pn)}/{n}}
so that

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},s\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({X^{*}})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({X^{*}})\leq s\,|\,\xi_{1:n})-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({X})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({X})\leq s)\right|

(5.19)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathbb{E}[\max_{\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\rm p}}|X_{\ell}-\mathbb{E}(X_{\ell})|/\sigma_{\ell}]}{C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}}\left(\frac{B_{n}^{2}\log^{3}(pn)}{n}\right)^{1/4}\lesssim\nu_{n,1},

where
C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}
is defined in Condition
3.1
. Hence (
5.15
) holds under the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
.

5.4.2
Proof of Claim
5.2

Define
Y^{\prime}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\xi_{i}^{\prime}-\mu+a)
, where
\xi_{1:n}^{\prime}
are independent copies of
\xi_{1:n}
. Then
Y^{\prime}
and
Y
are independently and identically distributed. We show (
5.16
) by applying Lemma
5.2
to
X^{*}
and
Y^{\prime}
. We need to verify that the conditions of Lemma
5.2
are satisfied conditional on
\xi_{1:n}
under the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
. By Lemma 4.1 and Lemma 4.2 in
[
12
]
, we have that Conditions
5.1
-
5.3
and the moment conditions (
5.9
) and (
5.10
) are satisfied conditional on the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
. Therefore, we are only left with verifying Condition
5.4
for
X^{*}
.
Applying Claim
5.1
, under the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, for all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
, and
t\in(0,\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({X^{*}})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({X^{*}})\leq s+t\,|\,\xi_{1:n})\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({X})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({X})\leq s+t)+K_{0}\nu_{n,1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({X})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({X})\leq s)+K_{0}t\cdot\frac{\mathbb{E}[\max_{\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\rm p}}|X_{\ell}-\mathbb{E}(X_{\ell})|/\sigma_{\ell}]}{C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}}+K_{0}\nu_{n,1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({X^{*}})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({X^{*}})\leq s\,|\,\xi_{1:n})+K_{0}t\cdot\frac{\mathbb{E}[\max_{\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\rm p}}|X_{\ell}-\mathbb{E}(X_{\ell})|/\sigma_{\ell}]}{C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}}+2K_{0}\nu_{n,1},

where
K_{0}>0
is a large enough constant; the first and the third inequalities hold by (
5.19
), and the second inequality holds by applying
Theorem
2.4
to
X
since
X
is a Gaussian random vector. Then we have that Condition
5.4
is satisfied by
X^{*}
with
r_{n}={\mathbb{E}[\max_{\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\rm p}}|X_{\ell}-\mathbb{E}(X_{\ell})|/\sigma_{\ell}]}/{C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}}
and
\varepsilon_{n}=\nu_{n,1}
for the constant
C=2K_{0}
.

Therefore, applying Lemma
5.2
, under the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},s\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({X^{*}})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({X^{*}})\leq s\,|\,\xi_{1:n})-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({Y})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({Y})\leq s)\right|

\displaystyle\quad=\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},s\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({X^{*}})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({X^{*}})\leq s\,|\,\xi_{1:n})-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({Y^{\prime}})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({Y^{\prime}})\leq s\,|\,\xi_{1:n})\right|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\nu_{n,1},

where
Y^{\prime}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\xi_{i}^{\prime}-\mu+a)
with
\xi_{1:n}^{\prime}
being independent copies of
\xi_{1:n}
. This completes the proof of Claim
5.2
.

5.4.3
Proof of Claim
5.3

Note that the Stein kernel for a Gaussian random vector is its covariance matrix. Then, by similar arguments of showing (
5.19
) and Lemma
5.1
, conditional on the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},s\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({X})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}({X})\leq s)-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}({\widehat{X}})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(\widehat{X})\leq s\,|\,\xi_{1:n})\right|

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\frac{\mathbb{E}[\max_{\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\rm p}}|X_{\ell}-\mathbb{E}(X_{\ell})|/\sigma_{\ell}]}{C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}}\Big(\log p\cdot\max_{j,k\in[p]}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\xi_{ij}-\bar{\xi}_{j})(\xi_{ik}-\bar{\xi}_{k})-\Sigma_{jk}^{*}\Big|\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\nu_{n,1},

and Claim
5.3
follows.

6
Conclusion

To conclude, we derive new dimension-free anti-concentration bounds for the difference between the maxima of two Gaussian random vectors. In contrast to existing literature, our bounds depend only on pairwise correlations and not on the minimum eigenvalue of the covariance matrix.
In particular, under the homogeneous variance setting, our bound holds when there is no perfect positive correlation between the two random vectors. Meanwhile, for the heterogeneous variance setting, our bound holds under a mild covariance condition.
We also apply our new anti-concentration bounds to establish the central limit theorem for the maximizer of empirical processes indexed by a discrete set, and we propose a valid Gaussian multiplier approximation for the distribution of the maximizer.
We conduct extensive simulation studies to validate our findings empirically. Also, in our simulation studies, we find that the bounds may hold under more relaxed assumptions. We plan to further relax the assumptions in our future work.

Appendix A
Proofs of Technical Lemmas in the Main Text

We provide in Appendix
A
the proofs for technical lemmas in the main text. For notational simplicity, for two random variables
X_{1},X_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, we denote by
X_{1}\equiv X_{2}
when
X_{1}=X_{2}
almost surely.

A.1
Proof of Lemma
2.5

.

We start by proving that case (1). First, the absolute continuity for the joint distribution of
(M_{\mathcal{A}},M_{\mathcal{B}})=(\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}X_{i},\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}X_{j})
follows from the assumption that
|{\rm Corr}(X_{i},X_{j})|<1
for any
i\in\mathcal{A}
and
j\in\mathcal{B}
together with the fact that for any Borel measurable subset
\mathcal{I}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we have

\mathbb{P}((M_{\mathcal{A}},M_{\mathcal{B}})\in\mathcal{I})\leq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},j\in\mathcal{B}}\mathbb{P}((X_{i},X_{j})\in\mathcal{I}).

Next, we prove (
2.9
) by showing that

\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-2}\mathbb{P}(x<M_{\mathcal{A}}\leq x+\varepsilon,y<M_{\mathcal{B}}\leq y+\varepsilon)=f(x,y),\quad\text{for a.e. $(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}$.}

(A.1)

First, note that for any
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we have:

\{x<M_{\mathcal{A}}\leq x+\varepsilon,y<M_{\mathcal{B}}\leq y+\varepsilon\}=\cup_{q=1}^{|\mathcal{A}|}\cup_{k=1}^{|\mathcal{B}|}\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{q,k},

where the event
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{q,k}
is defined as

\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{q,k}=\left\{\exists I_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{A},|I_{1}|=q\text{ and }I_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{B},|I_{2}|=k:\begin{array}[]{ll}x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,&\,\,\forall i\in I_{1}\\
X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash I_{1}\\
y<X_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon,&\,\,\forall j\in I_{2}\\
X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y,&\,\,\forall j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash I_{2}\end{array}\right\}.

By construction, the sets
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{q,k}
’s are disjoint.
Then, we propose the following two claims.

Claim A.1
.

For a.e.
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-2}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1,1})=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},j\in\mathcal{B}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}}X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=x,X_{j}=y\Big)\phi_{i,j}(x,y).

Proof.

See Appendix
C.1.1
.
∎

Claim A.2
.

For
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{q,k}
with
q\geq 2
or
k\geq 2
,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{q,k})=o(\varepsilon^{2})
when
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
for a.e.
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Proof.

See Appendix
C.1.2
.
∎

Combining Claim
A.1
and Claim
A.2
, we have that, for a.e.
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-2}\mathbb{P}(x<M_{\mathcal{A}}\leq x+\varepsilon,y<M_{\mathcal{B}}\leq y+\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-2}\sum_{q\in[|\mathcal{A}|],k\in[|\mathcal{B}|]}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{q,k})

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-2}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1,1})=f(x,y),

which completes the proof of (
2.9
) for case (1).

Next, we show that case (2) holds. The absolute continuity follows from the condition that
\sigma_{i}>0
for any
i\in\mathcal{A}
, along with the bound that for any Borel measurable subset
\mathcal{I}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})
,

\mathbb{P}(M_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{I},M_{\mathcal{B}}\leq y)\leq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}(X_{i}\in\mathcal{I},M_{\mathcal{B}}\leq y)\leq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}(X_{i}\in\mathcal{I})\leq|\mathcal{A}|.

Then, similar to the proof of case (1), to show that (
2.10
) holds, we only need to show that

\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-1}\mathbb{P}(x<M_{\mathcal{A}}\leq x+\varepsilon,M_{\mathcal{B}}\leq y)=f_{y}(x),\quad\text{for a.e. $x\in\mathbb{R}$ given any $y\in\mathbb{R}$,}

(A.2)

where
f_{y}(x):=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}X_{j}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=x\Big)\phi_{i}(x)
.
First, we have the following disjoint decomposition
that

\{x<M_{\mathcal{A}}\leq x+\varepsilon,M_{\mathcal{B}}\leq y\}=\cup_{k=1}^{|\mathcal{A}|}\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{k},

where each event
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{k}
is defined as

\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{k}=\left\{\exists I\subseteq\mathcal{A},|I|=k:\begin{array}[]{ll}x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,&\,\,\forall i\in I\\
X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash I\\
X_{j}\leq y,&\,\,\forall j\in\mathcal{B}\end{array}\right\}.

Then, similar to the proof of case (1), the proof of (
A.2
) depends on the following two claims.

Claim A.3
.

Given any
y\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-1}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1})=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}X_{j}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=x\Big)\phi_{i}(x)=f_{y}(x),

for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

See Appendix
C.1.3
.
∎

Claim A.4
.

Given any
y\in\mathbb{R}
, for
k\geq 2
,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{k})=o(\varepsilon)
as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

See Appendix
C.1.4
.
∎

Combining Claim
A.3
and Claim
A.4
, for any given
y\in\mathbb{R}
, we have that, for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-1}\mathbb{P}(x<M_{\mathcal{A}}\leq x+\varepsilon,M_{\mathcal{B}}\leq y)=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{|\mathcal{A}|}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{k})=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-1}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1})=f_{y}(x).

Consequently, (
A.2
) holds, which completes the proof of (
2.10
).
∎

A.2
Proof of Lemma
2.6

.

We show that (
2.14
) and (
2.15
) in Lemma
2.6
hold
for a.e.
u\in\mathbb{R}
and every
j\in\mathcal{B}
. For a given
j\in\mathcal{B}
, let

{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}:=\{i\in[p]\backslash\{j\}:1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}>0\},\quad{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}:=\{i\in[p]\backslash\{j\}:1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}=0\},

and

\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}:={\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\cap\{i\in[p]\backslash\{j\}:|{\rm Corr}(X_{i},X_{j})|=1\},

(A.3)

where by assumption we have that
\mathcal{A}\subseteq{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle H_{u,j}

\displaystyle(x)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i\in\mathcal{A},j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}}X_{i}\vee(X_{j^{\prime}}-u)\leq x|X_{j}=x+u\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(X_{i}\leq x+u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\},\,\,i\in[p]\backslash\{j\}|X_{j}=x+u\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(V_{i}\leq x+u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}-\mu_{i}-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(x+u-\mu_{j}),\,i\in[p]\backslash\{j\}|\,X_{j}=x+u\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\rm(a)}{=}\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\left(V_{i}-u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}+\mu_{i}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j})\right)\leq x,&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\\
V_{i}\leq u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}-\mu_{i}-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j}),&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{\rm(b)}{=}\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\!\left(V_{i}-u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}+\mu_{i}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j})\right)\leq x,&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\\
(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\left(\mu_{i}-u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j})\right)\leq x,&\,i\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\\
V_{i}\leq u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}-\mu_{i}-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j}),&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{\rm(c)}{=}\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\left(V_{i}+\mu_{i}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j})\right)\leq x,&\,i\in\mathcal{A}\\
(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\left(V_{i}+\mu_{i}-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}\mu_{j}\right)-u\leq x,&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\backslash\mathcal{A}\\
(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\left(\mu_{i}-u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j})\right)\leq x,&\,i\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\\
V_{i}\leq u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}-\mu_{i}-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j}){,}&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}\end{array}\right\},

where
V_{i}=X_{i}-\mu_{i}-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(X_{j}-\mu_{j})
is the orthogonal residual of
X_{i}
independent of
X_{j}
. Equality (a) follows from the fact that
[p]\backslash\{j\}={\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}
when condition (A) holds, equality (b) separates variables perfectly correlated with
X_{j}
that are constants conditional on
X_{j}=x+u
, and equality (c) follows from the assumption that
\max_{i\in\mathcal{A},j\in\mathcal{B}}|\operatorname{Corr}(X_{i},X_{j})|<1
such that
\mathcal{A}\subseteq{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}
.
When
\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\neq\varnothing
, we let

x_{j}(u)=\max_{i\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}}(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\left(\mu_{i}-u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j})\right),

and when
\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}=\varnothing
, we let
x_{j}(u)=-\infty
.

In what follows, we discuss the case when
x_{j}(u)>-\infty
, and the results for the case
x_{j}(u)=-\infty
follow by similar arguments. First, we observe that

(1)

when
x\geq x_{j}(u)
:

\displaystyle H_{u,j}(x)=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\left(V_{i}+\mu_{i}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j})\right)\leq x,&\,i\in\mathcal{A}\\
(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\left(V_{i}+\mu_{i}-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}\mu_{j}\right)-u\leq x,&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\backslash\mathcal{A}\\
V_{i}\leq u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}-\mu_{i}-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j}),&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}\end{array}\right\},

(2)

when
x<x_{j}(u)
:
H_{u,j}(x)=0
as there exists an
i\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}
that violates the constraint:

(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\left(\mu_{i}-u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j})\right)\leq x.

Moreover, recall the definition of
g_{u,j}(x)
is

g_{u,j}(x)=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A},j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}X_{i^{\prime}}\!\vee\!(X_{j^{\prime}}-u)\!\leq\!x\Big|X_{i}=x,X_{j}=x+u\Big)\phi_{i|j}(x|x+u),

where for any
i\in\mathcal{A}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A},j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}X_{i^{\prime}}\vee(X_{j^{\prime}}-u)\leq x\Big|X_{i}=x,X_{j}=x+u\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\},\,i^{\prime}\in[p]\backslash\cup\{i,j\}\Big|X_{i}=x,X_{j}=x+u\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\},&\,i^{\prime}\in[p]\backslash(\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\cup\{i,j\})\\
X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\},&\,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\end{array}\Bigg|X_{i}=x,X_{j}=x+u\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\!\!\left\{X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\},i^{\prime}\in[p]\backslash(\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\cup\{i,j\})\right\}\cap\left\{x\geq x_{j}(u)\right\}\,\Big|X_{i}=x,X_{j}=x+u\!\right).

The second equality follows from the fact that
\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\subseteq[p]\backslash\{i,j\}
by the assumption
\mathcal{A}\subseteq{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}
such that
i\notin\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}
for any
i\in\mathcal{A}
, and the third equality follows from the equivalence for all
i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}
, conditional on
X_{j}=x+u
:

\big\{X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\}\big\}=\Big\{(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j})^{-1}\Big(\mu_{i^{\prime}}-u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j}(u-\mu_{j})\Big)\leq x\Big\}.

Therefore,
g_{u,j}(x)=0=dH_{u,j}(x)/dx
when
x<x_{j}(u)
, and (
2.14
) follows.

Next, we move on to show that (
2.15
) holds when
x>x_{j}(u)
. We first rewrite
H_{u,j}(x)
as

\displaystyle H_{u,j}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\left(V_{i}+\mu_{i}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j})\right)\leq x,&\,i\in\mathcal{A}\\
(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\left(V_{i}+\mu_{i}-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}\mu_{j}\right)-u\leq x,&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\backslash\mathcal{A}\\
V_{i}\leq u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}-\mu_{i}-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j}),&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{V}_{i}\leq x,&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\\
\widetilde{U}_{i}\leq 0,&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}\end{array}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}}\widetilde{V}_{i}\leq x,\quad\max_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}}\widetilde{U}_{i}\leq 0\right),

where

\widetilde{V}_{i}=(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\left(V_{i}-u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}+\mu_{i}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j})\right),\quad i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1},

\text{and}\quad\widetilde{U}_{i}=V_{i}-u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}+\mu_{i}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j}),\quad i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0},

and the second equality follows from that since
\mathcal{A}\subseteq{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}
, we have
\mathcal{A}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\backslash\mathcal{A}={\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}
.

To apply (
2.10
) of Lemma
2.5
to compute the derivative of
H_{u,j}(x)
on
x
, we verify that the conditions in case (2) of Lemma
2.5
hold for a.e.
u\in\mathbb{R}
. First, by the construction of the set
\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}
in (
A.3
), we have that
\operatorname{Var}(\widetilde{V}_{i})>0
for any
i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}
. Next, we remove the duplicate terms in
\{\widetilde{V}_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}}
, so that for the remaining
i,j\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}
and
i\neq j
, it holds that either
\operatorname{Var}(\widetilde{V}_{i}-\widetilde{V}_{j})>0
or
\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{V}_{i}-\widetilde{V}_{j})\neq 0
. To do so, we show that
the duplicate terms only occur within the set
{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\backslash\mathcal{A}
:

Claim A.5
.

For a.e.
u\in\mathbb{R}
, we have the following hold

\widetilde{V}_{i}\not\equiv\widetilde{V}_{i^{\prime}},\quad\text{for any $i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}$ and $i\neq i^{\prime}$};

(A.4)

\widetilde{V}_{i}\not\equiv\widetilde{V}_{i^{\prime}},\quad\text{for any $i\in\mathcal{A}$ and $i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\backslash\mathcal{A}$}.

(A.5)

Proof.

See Appendix
C.2
.
∎

By Claim
A.5
, we have that for a.e.
u\in\mathbb{R}
, the identity
\widetilde{V}_{i}\equiv\widetilde{V}_{i^{\prime}}
for
i\neq i^{\prime}
can only occur to
i,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\backslash\mathcal{A}
.
We denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\subseteq{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\backslash\mathcal{A}
the subset with all duplicates of
\widetilde{V}_{i}
’s removed, i.e.,
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
is any subset of
{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\backslash\mathcal{A}
such that for any
i,i^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
and
i\neq i^{\prime}
, we have
\widetilde{V}_{i}\not\equiv\widetilde{V}_{i^{\prime}}
, and if
{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\backslash\mathcal{A}\backslash\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\neq\varnothing
, then for any
k\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}^{1}\backslash\mathcal{A}\backslash\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
, there exists a
k^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
such that
\widetilde{V}_{k}\equiv\widetilde{V}_{k^{\prime}}
.

Then we have that

\displaystyle H_{u,j}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{V}_{i}\leq x,&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}\\
\widetilde{U}_{i}\leq 0,&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}\end{array}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{V}_{i}\leq x,&\,i\in\mathcal{A}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\\
\widetilde{U}_{i}\leq 0,&\,i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}}\widetilde{V}_{i}\leq x,\quad\max_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}}\widetilde{U}_{i}\leq 0\right),

and

\displaystyle dH_{u,j}(x)/dx\overset{\rm(d)}{=}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}}\Bigg\{\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{V}_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\\
\widetilde{U}_{i^{\prime}}\leq 0,&\,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}\end{array}\Bigg|\widetilde{V}_{i}=x\right)f_{\widetilde{V}_{i}}(x)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\rm(e)}{=}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}}\Bigg\{\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{V}_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}\\
\widetilde{U}_{i^{\prime}}\leq 0,&\,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}\end{array}\Bigg|\widetilde{V}_{i}=x\right)f_{\widetilde{V}_{i}}(x)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}}\Bigg\{\mathbb{P}\!\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{V}_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}\\
\widetilde{U}_{i^{\prime}}\leq 0,&\,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}\\
x_{j}(u)\leq x&\end{array}\Bigg|\widetilde{V}_{i}=x\right)f_{\widetilde{V}_{i}}(x)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\rm(f)}{=}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}}\Bigg\{\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A},j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}X_{i^{\prime}}\vee(X_{j^{\prime}}-u)\leq x\Big|X_{i}=x+u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\},X_{j}=x+u\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})\phi_{i|j}(x+u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}|x+u)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\rm(g)}{\geq}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\!\Bigg\{\!\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A},j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}X_{i^{\prime}}\!\vee\!(X_{j^{\prime}}-u)\leq x\Big|X_{i}=x,X_{j}=x+u\Big)(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})\phi_{i|j}(x|x+u)\!\!\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\Big(1-\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}\Big)g_{u,j}(x),

where
f_{\widetilde{V}_{i}}(\cdot)
denotes the density function of
{\widetilde{V}_{i}}
; equality (d) follows from (
2.10
) of Lemma
2.5
by taking
y=0
, equality (e) follows by the fact that adding back the constraints of the duplicate terms does not change the probability, equality (f) follows from the fact that for any
i\in\mathcal{A}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
,
f_{\widetilde{V}_{i}}(x)=(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})\phi_{i|j}(x+u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\}|x+u)
and the fact that
\widetilde{U}_{i}
’s and
\widetilde{V}_{i}
’s are independent of
X_{j}
by construction, so that we have the equivalence:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\!\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{V}_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}\\
\widetilde{U}_{i^{\prime}}\leq 0,&\,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}\\
x_{j}(u)\leq x&\end{array}\Bigg|\widetilde{V}_{i}=x\right)=\mathbb{P}\!\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{V}_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}\\
\widetilde{U}_{i^{\prime}}\leq 0,&\,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{0}\\
x_{j}(u)\leq x&\end{array}\Bigg|\widetilde{V}_{i}=x,\,X_{j}=x+u\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A},j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}X_{i^{\prime}}\vee(X_{j^{\prime}}-u)\leq x\Big|X_{i}=x+u\cdot\mathbb{I}\{i\in\mathcal{B}\},X_{j}=x+u\Big),

and inequality (g) follows by noting that
1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}\geq 0
for all
i\in\mathcal{A}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
due to condition (A).
Hence (
2.15
) in Lemma
2.6
also follows, which completes the proof.
∎

A.3
Proof of Corollary
2.7

.

First observe that for any given
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\mathbb{P}(|\max_{j\in[p]}X_{j}-t|\leq\varepsilon)\leq\mathbb{P}(|\max_{j\in[p]}(X_{j}-t)/\sigma_{j}|\leq\varepsilon/\underline{\sigma}),

(A.6)

where
\underline{\sigma}=\min_{j\in[p]}\sigma_{j}
and the inequality follows from the fact that
|\max_{j\in[p]}\underline{\sigma}(X_{j}-t)/\sigma_{j}|\leq|\max_{j\in[p]}X_{j}-t|
. Then we will employ Theorem
2.4
to establish the single Gaussian maximum inequality by augmenting the scaled Gaussian random vector
(\widetilde{X}_{j}^{t})_{j=1}^{p}:=\big((X_{j}-t)/\sigma_{j}\big)_{j=1}^{p}
with i.i.d. standard normal random variables. Specifically, define the multivariate Gaussian random vector
(\widetilde{X}_{1}^{t},\ldots,\widetilde{X}_{p}^{t},\widetilde{X}_{p+1},\ldots,\widetilde{X}_{p+n})
, where
\widetilde{X}_{j}\overset{\rm i.i.d.}{\sim}{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
for
j\in[p+n]\backslash[p]
and
\widetilde{X}_{i}^{t}\perp\!\!\!\!\perp\widetilde{X}_{j}
for any
i\in[p]
and
j\in[p+n]\backslash[p]
.
Consider the partition
\mathcal{B}=[p]
and
\mathcal{A}=[p+n]\backslash[p]
, and define
M_{\mathcal{A}}=\max_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\widetilde{X}_{i}
and
M_{\mathcal{B}}=\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}\widetilde{X}_{j}^{t}
. By Theorem
2.4
, for all
\varepsilon>0
,
\delta>0
and
t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|M_{\mathcal{B}}-t^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\ \leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\!\Big\{\!|M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}}-t^{\prime}+\mathbb{E}M_{\mathcal{A}}|\!\leq\varepsilon+\frac{C}{\sqrt{\log n}}\Big\}\!\cap\!\Big\{|M_{\mathcal{A}}-\mathbb{E}M_{\mathcal{A}}|\!\leq\!\frac{C}{\sqrt{\log n}}\Big\}\!\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\!\mathbb{P}\Big(\!|M_{\mathcal{A}}-\mathbb{E}M_{\mathcal{A}}|>\!\frac{C}{\sqrt{\log n}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \leq\mathcal{L}\Big(M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon+\frac{C}{\sqrt{\log n}}\Big)+C^{-2}\log n\operatorname{Var}(M_{\mathcal{A}})

\displaystyle\ \leq(2+\delta)\mathbb{E}\Big[{\max_{j\in[p]}\big|\widetilde{X}_{j}^{t}-\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{X}_{j}^{t})\big|}\Big]{\varepsilon}=(2+\delta)\mathbb{E}\Big[{\max_{j\in[p]}|{X}_{j}-\mu_{j}|/\sigma_{j}}\Big]{\varepsilon},

(A.7)

where the first inequality follows from triangle inequality, the second inequality follows from the definition of the Lévy concentration function and Markov’s inequality, and
the last inequality holds by choosing
C>0
and
n
properly large together with the fact that
\log n\operatorname{Var}(M_{\mathcal{A}})=O(1)
as
M_{\mathcal{A}}
is the maximum of i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)

[
5
]
. Then taking the infimum over
\delta>0
, (
A.7
) yields

\sup_{t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}(|\max_{j\in[p]}\widetilde{X}_{j}^{t}-t^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon)\leq 2\mathbb{E}\big[{\max_{j\in[p]}|X_{j}-\mu_{j}|/\sigma_{j}}\big]{\varepsilon}.

(A.8)

Combining (
A.6
) and (
A.8
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}(|\max_{j\in[p]}X_{j}-t|\leq\varepsilon)\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}(|\max_{j\in[p]}\widetilde{X}_{j}^{t}|\leq\varepsilon/\underline{\sigma})\leq 2\mathbb{E}\big[{\max_{j\in[p]}|X_{j}-\mu_{j}|/\sigma_{j}}\big]{\varepsilon}/\underline{\sigma},

which completes the proof of (
2.16
).
∎

A.4
Proof of Lemma
5.1

.

The proof is a modification of
the proof of Theorem 3.2 in
[
12
]
. Specifically, for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we replace the smoothing function
m^{y}(x)
in equation (65) of
[
12
]
with
\varphi^{v}_{s}(x)
defined in (
A.15
) and setting
\beta=\delta^{-1}\log p
. Next, we replace the function
h^{y}(x;t)
in equation (72) of
[
12
]
as
h^{v}_{s,\delta}(x,t)
in (
A.19
). Then, we define the term

\mathcal{I}^{v}_{s}=\varphi^{v}_{s}(V)-\varphi^{v}_{s}(Z),

where without loss of generality, we assume that
V
and
Z
are independent. Following the arguments as in Step 2 of the proof for Theorem 3.2 in
[
12
]
, we let

\Psi(t)=\mathbb{E}[\varphi_{s}^{v}(\sqrt{t}V+\sqrt{1-t}Z)],\quad\text{for all }t\in[0,1].

By the multivariate Stein identity
[
42
]
, we have that

\displaystyle\Psi^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{j}\varphi_{s}^{v}(\sqrt{t}V+\sqrt{1-t}Z)\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}V_{j}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-t}}Z_{j}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j,k=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi_{s}^{v}(\sqrt{t}V+\sqrt{1-t}Z)\left(\tau_{jk}(V)-\Sigma_{jk}\right)\right].

Next, following the same arguments as in the proof of Theorem 3.2 in
[
12
]
together with (
C.25
) in Appendix
C.4
, we have that

\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi_{s}^{v}(\sqrt{t}V+\sqrt{1-t}Z)=\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi_{s}^{v}(\sqrt{t}V+\sqrt{1-t}Z)h^{v^{\prime}}_{s^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}}(Z,0),

(A.9)

where

v^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-t}}v+\sqrt{\frac{t}{1-t}}V,\quad s^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-t}}s,\quad\delta^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-t}}\delta.

Then, since
Z
and
V
are independent, and
Z
satisfies Condition
3.1
, by Theorem
2.1
and Theorem
2.4
, we have that

\mathbb{E}[h^{v^{\prime}}_{s^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}}(Z,0)|V=x]\leq\frac{K_{0}\mathbb{E}[\max_{\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\rm p}}|Z_{\ell}|/\sigma_{\ell}]}{C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}\sqrt{1-t}}\delta,\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{p},

where
K_{0}>0
is a universal constant.
Then we have that

\displaystyle|\Psi^{\prime}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j,k=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[h^{v^{\prime}}_{s^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}}(Z,0)\cdot\left|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi_{s}^{v}(\sqrt{t}V+\sqrt{1-t}Z)\right|\cdot\left|\tau_{jk}(V)-\Sigma_{jk}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{0}\delta^{-2}\log p}{2}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbb{E}[h^{v^{\prime}}_{s^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}}(Z,0)|V]\max_{j,k\in[p]}|\tau_{jk}(V)-\Sigma_{jk}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K_{0}C_{0}\delta^{-1}\log p\mathbb{E}[\max_{\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\rm p}}|Z_{\ell}|/\sigma_{\ell}]}{2C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}\sqrt{1-t}}\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{j,k\in[p]}|\tau_{jk}(V)-\Sigma_{jk}|\right),

where the first inequality follows from (
A.9
), and the second inequality follows by applying the derivative bound in (
A.17
) of Lemma
A.1
. In turn, we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{0}^{1}\Psi(t)dt\right|\leq\frac{K_{0}C_{0}\delta^{-1}\log p\mathbb{E}[\max_{\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\rm p}}|Z_{\ell}|/\sigma_{\ell}]\Delta}{2C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-t}}dt

\displaystyle=K_{0}C_{0}C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}^{-1}\delta^{-1}\log p\mathbb{E}[\textstyle\max_{\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\rm p}}|Z_{\ell}|/\sigma_{\ell}]\Delta.

Then, for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{j\in\mathcal{A}}(V_{j}+v_{j})-\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}(V_{j^{\prime}}+v_{j^{\prime}})\leq s\right)\leq\mathbb{P}(\zeta_{\beta}^{v}(V)\leq s+\delta)\leq\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{s+\delta}^{v}(V))

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{s+\delta}^{v}(Z))+|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|\leq\mathbb{P}(\zeta_{\beta}^{v}(Z)\leq s+2\delta)+|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\max_{j\in\mathcal{A}}(Z_{j}+v_{j})-\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}(Z_{j^{\prime}}+v_{j^{\prime}})\leq s+3\delta)+|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\max_{j\in\mathcal{A}}(Z_{j}+v_{j})-\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}(Z_{j^{\prime}}+v_{j^{\prime}})\leq s)+|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|+K_{1}C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\max_{\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\rm p}}|Z_{\ell}|/\sigma_{\ell}]\delta,

where the last but one inequality is a result of (
A.14
) and the choice of
\beta=\delta^{-1}\log p
, and the last inequality is a result of Theorem
2.1
and Theorem
2.4
, and
K_{1}>0
is a universal constant. Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{j\in\mathcal{A}}(V_{j}+v_{j})-\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}(V_{j^{\prime}}+v_{j^{\prime}})\leq s\right)\geq\mathbb{P}(\zeta_{\beta}^{v}(V)\leq s-\delta)\geq\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{s-2\delta}^{v}(V))

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{s-2\delta}^{v}(Z))-|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|\geq\mathbb{P}(\zeta_{\beta}^{v}(Z)\leq s-2\delta)-|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\max_{j\in\mathcal{A}}(Z_{j}+v_{j})-\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}(Z_{j^{\prime}}+v_{j^{\prime}})\leq s-3\delta)-|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\max_{j\in\mathcal{A}}(Z_{j}+v_{j})-\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}(Z_{j^{\prime}}+v_{j^{\prime}})\leq s)-|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|-K_{1}C_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\max_{\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\rm p}}|Z_{\ell}|/\sigma_{\ell}]\delta.

Then (
5.8
) holds by taking
\delta=\sqrt{\Delta\log p}
, which completes the proof.
∎

A.5
Proof of Lemma
5.2

.

The proof of Lemma
5.2
follows the proof of Theorem 3.1 in
[
12
]
by modifying Lemma 3.1 in
[
12
]
using redefined smoothing functions. For ease of presentation, we denote the constants in Conditions
5.1
-
5.4
as
C_{v}
,
C_{e}
,
C_{b}
, and
C_{a}
respectively, and we denote the constant in (
5.9
) and (
5.10
) as
C_{m}
. Then, for any
\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, we redefine the variable
\varrho_{\epsilon}
in (21) of
[
12
]
as

\varrho_{\epsilon}=\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},s\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(S_{n,\epsilon}^{V})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(S_{n,\epsilon}^{V})\leq s\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})\leq s\big)\right|,

where
S_{n,\epsilon}^{V}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\epsilon_{i}V_{i}+(1-\epsilon_{i})Z_{i}\big)
. We adopt the same sequence of random vectors
\epsilon^{0},\ldots,\epsilon^{D}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
as defined in Remark 3.1 of
[
12
]
and define
I_{d}=\{i\in[n]:\epsilon^{d}_{i}=1\}
for
d=0,\ldots,D
, where
D={\rm round}(4\log n)+1
, and
{\rm round}(\cdot)
is the rounding operator that rounds the number inside to the nearest integer. For all
i\in[n]
and
j,k,\ell\in[p]
, we let

\begin{gathered}\mathcal{E}_{i,jk}^{V}=\mathbb{E}\left[V_{ij}V_{ik}\right],\quad\mathcal{E}_{i,jk\ell}^{V}=\mathbb{E}\left[V_{ij}V_{ik}V_{i\ell}\right],\\
\mathcal{E}_{i,jk}^{Z}=\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{ij}Z_{ik}\right],\quad\mathcal{E}_{i,jk\ell}^{Z}=\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{ij}Z_{ik}Z_{i\ell}\right],\end{gathered}

and for
n\geq 1
and
d=0,\ldots,D
, we consider the event
\mathcal{A}_{d}
defined in Remark 3.1 of
[
12
]
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{d}=

\displaystyle\Big\{\max_{j,k\in[p]}\!\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\!\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}^{d}\!\!\left(\!\mathcal{E}_{i,jk}^{V}\!-\mathcal{E}_{i,jk}^{Z}\right)\!\!\Big|\!\leq\mathcal{B}_{n,1,d}\!\Big\}\!\bigcap\!\Big\{\max_{j,k,\ell\in[p]}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\!\sum_{i=1}^{n}\!\epsilon_{i}^{d}\!\!\left(\mathcal{E}_{i,jk\ell}^{V}-\mathcal{E}_{i,jk\ell}^{Z}\right)\!\!\Big|\!\leq\mathcal{B}_{n,2,d}\!\Big\},

where
\mathcal{B}_{n,1,d},\mathcal{B}_{n,2,d}>0
are some positive rates to be specified later.

Then, we finish the proof in two steps.

In Step 1, we first establish a recursive inequality of
\rho_{\epsilon^{d}}
, which is a counterpart of the rate in Lemma 3.1 of
[
12
]
in our case. Specifically, for any
d=0,\ldots,D
and any constant
\delta>0
such that

C_{e}B_{n}\delta^{-1}\log^{2}(pn)\leq\sqrt{n},

(A.10)

we show that the following holds under event
\mathcal{A}_{d}
,

\displaystyle\varrho_{\epsilon^{d}}\lesssim

\displaystyle r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{5}(pn)}{n^{2}}

(A.11)

\displaystyle+\big(r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}}|\epsilon^{d})\big)\left(\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n,1,d}\delta^{-2}\log p}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n,2,d}\delta^{-3}\log^{2}p}{{n}}+\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{3}(pn)}{n}\right).

Then by the proof of Corollary 3.1 in
[
12
]
, there exists a constant
K_{\rm iter}>0
depending only on
C_{v}
,
C_{e}
and
C_{b}
such that by taking

\mathcal{B}_{n,1,d+1}\geq\mathcal{B}_{n,1,d}+K_{\rm iter}B_{n}\log^{1/2}(pn)\text{ and }\mathcal{B}_{n,2,d+1}\geq\mathcal{B}_{n,2,d}+K_{\rm iter}B_{n}^{2}\log^{3/2}(pn),

(A.12)

we have a recursive inequality for
\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d}}\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{A}_{d}\})
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d}}\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{A}_{d}\})\lesssim

\displaystyle r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{5}(pn)}{n^{2}}+\big(r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}}\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{A}_{d+1}\})\big)

(A.13)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n,1,d}\delta^{-2}\log p}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n,2,d}\delta^{-3}\log^{2}p}{{n}}+\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{3}(pn)}{n}\right).

In Step 2, following (
A.13
), we employ an induction argument and follow the proof of Theorem 3.1 in
[
12
]
to show that (
5.11
) holds.

For the rest of the proof, same as in
[
12
]
, for ease of notation, we denote
\mathbb{P}(\cdot)
and
\mathbb{E}(\cdot)
as
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|\epsilon^{d})
and
\mathbb{E}(\cdot|\epsilon^{d})
respectively, and we let
\lesssim
be that the inequality holds up to a constant depending only on
C_{v}
,
C_{e}
,
C_{b}
,
C_{a}
and
C_{\gamma}
.

Step 1

Similar to the proof of Lemma 3.1 of
[
12
]
, to show (
A.11
), we begin by constructing a smoothing approximation function of
\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(\cdot)-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(\cdot)\leq s)
for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. In particular, given any
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\delta>0
, we find a five-times continuously differentiable function
\gamma^{0}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,1]
with derivatives bounded up to the fifth order such that
\gamma^{0}(t)=1
for any
t\leq 0
and
\gamma^{0}(t)=0
for any
t\geq 1
. Then for the given
\delta>0
, we define
\gamma_{s}(t)=\gamma_{s,\delta}(t)=\gamma^{0}(\delta^{-1}(t-s))
. It is not difficult to show that for any
d=1,\ldots,5
, there exists an absolute constant
C_{\gamma}\geq 1
such that
\|\gamma_{s}^{(d)}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{\gamma}\delta^{-d}
, where
\gamma_{s}^{(d)}
denotes the
d
-th derivative of
\gamma_{s}
, and
\mathbb{I}\{t\leq s\}\leq\gamma_{s}(t)\leq\mathbb{I}\{t\leq s+\delta\}
for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
. For any vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, define the function
\zeta_{\beta}^{v}:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as

\zeta_{\beta}^{v}(x)=\zeta_{\beta,\mathcal{A}}^{v}(x)=\beta^{-1}\big(\log\big(\textstyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{A}}e^{\beta(x_{j}+v_{j})}\big)-\log\big(\textstyle\sum_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}e^{\beta(x_{j^{\prime}}+v_{j^{\prime}})}\big)\big),

and it also holds that

-\beta^{-1}\log p\leq\zeta_{\beta}^{v}(x)-\{\max_{j\in\mathcal{A}}(x_{j}+v_{j})-\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}(x_{j^{\prime}}+v_{j^{\prime}})\}\leq\beta^{-1}\log p.

(A.14)

Then, we define the smoothing function as

\varphi^{v}_{s}(x)=\varphi_{s,\beta}^{v}(x)=\gamma_{s}(\zeta_{\beta}^{v}(x)).

(A.15)

The following lemma bounds the partial derivatives of
\varphi^{v}_{s}
:

Lemma A.1
.

Letting
\beta=\delta^{-1}\log p
, for any
j,k,\ell,r,h\in[p]
, there exists functions
U_{jk}(x):\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
U_{jk\ell}(x):\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
U_{jk\ell r}(x):\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
U_{jk\ell rh}(x):\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and universal constants
C_{0},C_{1},c_{1}>0
such that for any given
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, and for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, we have that

\displaystyle|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi^{v}_{s}(x)|\leq U_{jk}(x),

\displaystyle|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\partial_{\ell}\varphi^{v}_{s}(x)|\leq U_{jk\ell}(x),

(A.16)

\displaystyle|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\partial_{\ell}\partial_{r}\varphi^{v}_{s}(x)|\leq U_{jk\ell r}(x),

\displaystyle|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\partial_{\ell}\partial_{r}\partial_{h}\varphi^{v}_{s}(x)|\leq U_{jk\ell rh}(x),

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k=1}^{p}U_{jk}(x)\leq C_{0}\delta^{-2}\log p,

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k,\ell=1}^{p}U_{jk\ell}(x)\leq C_{0}\delta^{-3}\log^{2}p,

(A.17)

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k,\ell,r=1}^{p}U_{jk\ell r}(x)\leq C_{0}\delta^{-4}\log^{3}p,

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k,\ell,r,h=1}^{p}U_{jk\ell rh}(x)\leq C_{0}\delta^{-5}\log^{4}p.

Also, for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
such that
\max_{j\in[p]}|y_{j}|\leq\beta^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle c_{1}U_{jk\ell r}(x)\leq U_{jk\ell r}

\displaystyle(x+y)\leq C_{1}U_{jk\ell r}(x),

(A.18)

\displaystyle c_{1}U_{jk\ell rh}(x)\leq U_{jk\ell rh}

\displaystyle(x+y)\leq C_{1}U_{jk\ell rh}(x).

Proof.

See Appendix
C.3
.
∎

Then, choosing
\beta=\delta^{-1}\log p
and
\delta
that satisfies (
A.10
), we replace the function
m^{y}(\cdot)
in
[
12
]
by
\varphi^{v}_{s}(\cdot)
.If
\delta>1
, then
r_{n}\delta>1
and (
A.11
) holds trivially. Hence, we assume
\delta\leq 1
without loss of generality for the rest of the proof. We redefine the function in equation (72) in the proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
12
]
as

h^{v}_{s,\delta}(x,t)\!:=\mathbb{I}\{-\delta-t<\max_{j\in\mathcal{A}}(x_{j}\!+\!v_{j})\!-\!\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}(x_{j^{\prime}}\!+\!v_{j^{\prime}})\!-\!s\leq 2\delta+t\},\,x\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{p},\,t\geq 0,

(A.19)

and we adopt the same notation that
W=S_{n,\epsilon^{d+1}}^{V}
as defined in (73) of
[
12
]
. We redefine the error term in (71) of
[
12
]
as

\mathcal{I}^{v}_{s}=\varphi_{s}^{v}(S^{V}_{n,\epsilon^{d}})-\varphi_{s}^{v}(S^{Z}_{n}).

(A.20)

Then, to show (
A.11
), we first establish in the following claim the counterpart of (74) in
[
12
]
.

Claim A.6
.

For
d=0,\ldots,D
and the term
\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v}
defined in (
A.20
), when
\mathcal{A}_{d}
holds, we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},s\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{5}(pn)}{n^{2}}+\big(r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}})\big)

(A.21)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n,1,d}\delta^{-2}\log p}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n,2,d}\delta^{-3}\log^{2}p}{{n}}+\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{3}(pn)}{n}\right).

Proof.

See Appendix
C.4
.
∎

Now applying the properties of the functions
\gamma_{s}
and
\zeta_{\beta}^{v}
, for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(S_{n,\epsilon^{d}}^{V})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(S_{n,\epsilon^{d}}^{V})\leq s\right)\leq\mathbb{P}(\zeta_{\beta}^{v}(S_{n,\epsilon^{d}}^{V})\leq s+\delta)\leq\mathbb{E}\big(\varphi_{s+\delta}^{v}(S_{n,\epsilon^{d}}^{V})\big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{s+\delta}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z}))+|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|\leq\mathbb{P}(\zeta_{\beta}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})\leq s+2\delta)+|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})\leq s+3\delta)+|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})\leq s)+|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|+C_{a}(3r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}),

where the last inequality holds by Condition
5.4
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(S_{n,\epsilon^{d}}^{V})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(S_{n,\epsilon^{d}}^{V})\leq s\right)\geq\mathbb{P}(\zeta_{\beta}^{v}(S_{n,\epsilon^{d}}^{V})\leq s-\delta)\geq\mathbb{E}\big(\varphi_{s-2\delta}^{v}(S_{n,\epsilon^{d}}^{V})\big)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{s-2\delta}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z}))-|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|\geq\mathbb{P}(\zeta_{\beta}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})\leq s-2\delta)-|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|

\displaystyle\quad\geq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})\leq s-3\delta)-|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|

\displaystyle\quad\geq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})-\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{B}}^{v}(S_{n}^{Z})\leq s)-|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|-C_{a}(3r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}).

Taking the supremum over
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we have that

\varrho_{\epsilon^{d}}\lesssim\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},s\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{I}_{s}^{v})|+r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n},

which along with (
A.21
) gives (
A.11
), and (
A.13
) follows consequently by Corollary 3.1 of
[
12
]
.

Step 2

Following the proof of Theorem 3.1 in
[
12
]
, we employ (
A.13
) to show (
5.11
) through an inductive arguments. Let
\mathcal{B}_{n,1,d}
and
\mathcal{B}_{n,2,d}
be the same as (30) of
[
12
]
that

\mathcal{B}_{n,1,d}=C_{1}(d+1)B_{n}\log^{1/2}(pn)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{B}_{n,2,d}=C_{1}(d+1)B_{n}^{2}\log^{3/2}(pn),

where
C_{1}=C_{m}+K_{\rm iter}
, and recall that
K_{\rm iter}
is the constant in (
A.12
).
For each
d=0,1,\ldots,D
, we redefine

f_{d}=\inf\Big\{t\geq 1:\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d}}\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{A}_{d}))\leq t\Big(r_{n}\Big(\frac{B_{n}^{2}\log^{3}(pn)}{n}\Big)^{1/4}+\varepsilon_{n}\Big)\Big\},

and take

\delta=\delta_{d}=\frac{B_{n}^{1/2}\log^{3/4}(pn)((d+1)f_{d+1})^{1/3}}{n^{1/4}},

which satisfies (
A.10
) under the condition
C_{e}B_{n}^{2}\log^{5}(pn)\leq n
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{5}(pn)}{n^{2}}\leq\frac{\log^{2}(pn)}{n}\leq r_{n}\left(\frac{B_{n}^{2}\log^{3}(pn)}{n}\right)^{1/4},

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n,1,d}\delta^{-2}\log p}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\frac{C_{1}(d+1)^{1/3}}{f_{d+1}^{2/3}},\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n,2,d}\delta^{-3}\log^{2}p}{{n}}\bigvee\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{3}(pn)}{n}\leq\frac{C_{1}\vee 1}{f_{d+1}}.

Combining the above rates with (
A.13
) and following identical arguments as in the proof of Theorem 3.1 in
[
12
]
, we have that (
5.11
) holds as desired.
∎

Appendix B
Lemmas in Section
5.1

Lemma
B.1
characterizes the joint density of
(M_{\mathcal{A}},M_{\mathcal{B}})
when there exist perfectly negatively correlated pairs between
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
such that the joint distribution is not absolutely continuous everywhere on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Lemma
B.2
provides the anti-concentration rate for
M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}}
based on Lemma
B.1
. Lemma
B.3
establishes the one-to-one relationship between the sets
{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}
defined in (
5.1
), ensuring that Lemma
B.2
is applicable. The proofs of Lemmas
B.1
-
B.3
are in Sections
B.1
,
B.2
and
B.3
respectively.

Lemma B.1
.

Suppose that
(Z_{1},Z_{2},\ldots,Z_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a Gaussian random vector with mean
(\mu_{1},...,\mu_{d})^{\top}
, homogeneous component-wise variance
\sigma^{2}>0
, and
|\operatorname{Corr}(Z_{i},Z_{j})|<1
for all
i\neq j
. Define
M_{\mathcal{A}}=\max_{i\in[d]}Z_{i}
and
M_{\mathcal{B}}=\max_{i\in[d]}\{-Z_{i}+c_{i}\}
, where
c_{i}
’s are constants, and let
\bar{c}=\max_{i\in[d]}c_{i}
and
\mathcal{I}^{*}=\mathop{\text{\rm arg\,max}}_{i\in[d]}c_{i}
. Then, we have

(1)

Let
\mathcal{L}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:y=-x+\bar{c}\}
. The joint distribution of
(M_{\mathcal{A}},M_{\mathcal{B}})
is absolutely continuous on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\mathcal{L}
with respect to the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and a version of its density is

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}(x,y)=\sum_{(i,j)\in[d]^{2}:i\neq j}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\!\left(\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\\
\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}-Z_{j^{\prime}}+c_{j^{\prime}}\leq y\end{array}\!\bigg|Z_{i}=x,Z_{j}=-y+c_{j}\!\right)

(B.1)

\displaystyle\times\phi_{i,j}(x,-y+c_{j}),

where
\phi_{i,j}(\cdot,\cdot)
is the joint density for
(Z_{i},Z_{j})
.

(2)

On the line
\mathcal{L}
defined above, consider the distribution of
M_{\mathcal{A}}
when
(M_{\mathcal{A}},M_{\mathcal{B}})=(M_{\mathcal{A}},-M_{\mathcal{A}}+\bar{c})
. We have that the distribution of
M_{\mathcal{A}}
on
\mathcal{L}
is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}
, and a version of its density is

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}(x)=\frac{d}{dx}\mathbb{P}(M_{\mathcal{A}}\leq x,M_{\mathcal{B}}=-M_{\mathcal{A}}+\bar{c})

(B.2)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbb{P}(x+c_{i}-\bar{c}\leq Z_{i}\leq x,\forall i\neq i^{*}|Z_{i^{*}}=x)\phi_{i^{*}}(x),&\,\text{if $|\mathcal{I}^{*}|=1$ and $\mathcal{I}^{*}=\{i^{*}\}$},\\
0,&\,\text{if $|\mathcal{I}^{*}|>1$}.\end{array}\right.

where
\phi_{i}(\cdot)
is the marginal density for
Z_{i}
.

Lemma B.2
.

Let
(Z_{1},Z_{2},\ldots,Z_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a Gaussian random vector with mean
(\mu_{1},...,\mu_{d})^{\top}
and homogeneous component-wise variance
\sigma^{2}>0
. Also, assume that
|\operatorname{Corr}(Z_{i},Z_{j})|<1
for any
i\neq j
. Let
M_{\mathcal{A}}=\max_{i\in[d]}Z_{i}
and
M_{\mathcal{B}}=\max_{i\in[d]}\{-Z_{i}+c_{i}\}
, where
c_{i}
’s are constants.
Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, we have that

\mathcal{L}(M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon)\leq 3\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{i\in[d]}|Z_{i}-\mu_{i}|/\sigma\right)\frac{\varepsilon}{(1+\underline{\rho})\sigma},

(B.3)

where
\underline{\rho}=\min_{i\neq j}\operatorname{Corr}(Z_{i},Z_{j})
.

Lemma B.3
.

Let
(X_{1},\ldots,X_{p})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a Gaussian random vector such that
\operatorname{Corr}(X_{i},X_{j})<1
for any
i\neq j
. Let
\{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\}
be a nontrivial partition of
[p]
, and let
{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}
be defined in (
5.1
). Then, we have
|{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}|=|{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}|=d
for some integer
d\leq p
, and there exist constants
c_{1},\ldots,c_{d}
such that

\{X_{j}\}_{j\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\{-X_{i}+c_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}}.

(B.4)

Moreover, we have
|\operatorname{Corr}(X_{i},X_{i^{\prime}})|<1
for any
i,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
i\neq i^{\prime}
, and
|\operatorname{Corr}(X_{j},X_{j^{\prime}})|<1
for any
j,j^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}
and
j\neq j^{\prime}
.

B.1
Proof of Lemma
B.1

.

For
k\in[d]
, define the line
\mathcal{L}_{k}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:y=-x+c_{k}\}
. Then, we prove the results (1) and (2) of Lemma
B.1
by characterizing the densities on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})
and
\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k}
respectively.

B.1.1
Distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})

.

First, we show the absolute continuity of the joint distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})
. For
any Borel measurable subset
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left((M_{\mathcal{A}},M_{\mathcal{B}})\in\mathcal{I}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(i,j)\in[d]^{2}:i\neq j}\mathbb{P}((Z_{i},-Z_{j}+c_{j})\in\mathcal{I})+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{P}((Z_{i},-Z_{i}+c_{i})\in\mathcal{I})

\displaystyle=\sum_{(i,j)\in[d]^{2}:i\neq j}\mathbb{P}((Z_{i},-Z_{j}+c_{j})\in\mathcal{I}),

where the equality holds because
\mathbb{P}((Z_{i},-Z_{i}+c_{i})\in\mathcal{I})=0
for any
i\in[d]
and
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})
. Combined with the fact that
(Z_{i},Z_{j})
is non-degenerate for any
i\neq j
, the absolute continuity follows.

Next, we show that for a.e.
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})
, we have

\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-2}\mathbb{P}(x<M_{\mathcal{A}}\leq x+\varepsilon,y<M_{\mathcal{B}}\leq y+\varepsilon)=\widetilde{f}(x,y).

(B.5)

For any
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})
, we have

\{x<M_{\mathcal{A}}\leq x+\varepsilon,y<M_{\mathcal{B}}\leq y+\varepsilon\}=\cup_{p=1}^{d}\cup_{q=1}^{d}\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{p,q},

where the event
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{p,q}
is defined as

\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{p,q}=\left\{\exists I_{1}\subseteq[d],|I_{1}|=p\text{ and }I_{2}\subseteq[d],|I_{2}|=q:\begin{array}[]{ll}x<Z_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,&\,\,\forall i\in I_{1},\\
Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\notin I_{1},\\
y<-Z_{j}+c_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon,&\,\,\forall j\in I_{2},\\
-Z_{j^{\prime}}+c_{j^{\prime}}\leq y,&\,\,\forall j^{\prime}\notin I_{2}.\end{array}\right\},

and
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{p,q}
’s are disjoint. Then (
B.5
) follows by showing

\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-2}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1,1})=\widetilde{f}(x,y),\quad\text{for a.e. $(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})$;}

(B.6)

When either
p>1
or
q>1
,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{p,q})=o(\varepsilon^{2})
, for a.e.
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})
.

(B.7)

We show (
B.6
) first. For
p=q=1
and
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})
, we have the disjoint decomposition

\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1,1}\!=\!\!\!\displaystyle\bigcup_{i\in[d]}\displaystyle\bigcup_{j\in[d]}\!\!\!\left\{\!\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}\!Z_{i^{\prime}}\!\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\!\!-Z_{j^{\prime}}\!+\!c_{j^{\prime}}\!\leq\!y,x<\!Z_{i}\!\leq x\!+\!\varepsilon,y<\!-Z_{j}\!+\!c_{j}\!\leq y+\varepsilon\!\right\}\!,

and note that for
i=1,\ldots,d
, for small enough
\varepsilon>0
, by
(x,y)\notin\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}-Z_{i^{\prime}}+c_{i^{\prime}}\leq y,x<Z_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,y<-Z_{i}+c_{i}\leq y+\varepsilon\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{x<Z_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,y<-Z_{i}+c_{i}\leq y+\varepsilon\right\}=0.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1,1})

\displaystyle=\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(i,j)\in[d]^{2}:i\neq j}\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{P}\!\!\left\{\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}\!Z_{i^{\prime}}\!\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\!\!\!-Z_{j^{\prime}}\!+\!c_{j^{\prime}}\!\leq y,x<Z_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,y\!<\!-Z_{j}+c_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon\!\right\}

\displaystyle=\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(i,j)\in[d]^{2}:i\neq j}\int_{x}^{x+\varepsilon}\!\int_{y}^{y+\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\!\!\!-Z_{j^{\prime}}+c_{j^{\prime}}\leq y|Z_{i}=u,Z_{j}\!=\!-v+c_{j}\!\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\phi_{i,j}(u,-v+c_{j})dudv.

Then, we show that for a.e.
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})
, the following holds for any
i,j\in[d]
and
i\neq j
,

\displaystyle\lim_{u\downarrow x,v\downarrow y}\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}-Z_{j^{\prime}}+c_{j^{\prime}}\leq y|Z_{i}=u,Z_{j}=-v+c_{j}\right\}

(B.8)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}-Z_{j^{\prime}}+c_{j^{\prime}}\leq y|Z_{i}=x,Z_{j}=-y+c_{j}\right\}.

For
i^{\prime}\neq i
and
j^{\prime}\neq j
, we first define the residuals of orthogonal projections of
Z_{i^{\prime}}
and
-Z_{j^{\prime}}+c_{j^{\prime}}
onto
(Z_{i},-Z_{j}+c_{j})
as

V_{i^{\prime}}=Z_{i^{\prime}}-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(Z_{i}-\mu_{i})+a_{i^{\prime}j}(Z_{j}-\mu_{j}),

\text{and}\quad U_{j^{\prime}}=-Z_{j^{\prime}}+\mu_{j^{\prime}}+a_{j^{\prime}i}(Z_{i}-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(Z_{j}-\mu_{j}),

where for any
\ell\in[d]
, we define
(a_{\ell i},a_{\ell j})
as,

\displaystyle(a_{\ell i},a_{\ell j})^{\top}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{E}[(Z_{i}-\mu_{i},-Z_{j}+\mu_{j})^{\top}(Z_{i}-\mu_{i},-Z_{j}+\mu_{j})]\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}[(Z_{i}-\mu_{i},-Z_{j}+\mu_{j})^{\top}(Z_{\ell}-\mu_{\ell})].

Correspondingly, we define the set
\mathcal{D}_{i,j}=\mathcal{D}_{i,j}^{1}\cup\mathcal{D}_{i,j}^{2}
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{i,j}^{1}

\displaystyle=\cup_{i^{\prime}:V_{i^{\prime}}\equiv 0}\left\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:(1-a_{i^{\prime}i})x-a_{i^{\prime}j}y-\mu_{i^{\prime}}+a_{i^{\prime}i}\mu_{i}-a_{i^{\prime}j}(\mu_{j}-c_{j})=0\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{i,j}^{2}

\displaystyle=\cup_{j^{\prime}:U_{j^{\prime}}\equiv 0}\left\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:a_{j^{\prime}i}x+(1+a_{j^{\prime}j})y+\mu_{j^{\prime}}-c_{j^{\prime}}-a_{j^{\prime}i}\mu_{i}+a_{j^{\prime}j}(\mu_{j}-c_{j})=0\right\}.

We show that the set
\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
has a Lebesgue measure of 0 on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and (
B.8
) holds for any
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
.

We first show that
(1-a_{i^{\prime}i},-a_{i^{\prime}j})\neq\mathbf{0}
and
(a_{j^{\prime}i},1+a_{j^{\prime}j})\neq\mathbf{0}
for any
i^{\prime}\neq i
and
j^{\prime}\neq j
such that
V_{i^{\prime}}
and
U_{j^{\prime}}
degenerate to 0, which implies that
\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
is a union of lines or empty sets that has a Lebesgue measure of 0 in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. For
i^{\prime}\neq i
such that
V_{i^{\prime}}\equiv 0
, we have that
a_{i^{\prime}j}\neq 0
,
because otherwise
Z_{i}
and
Z_{i^{\prime}}
are perfectly correlated, which contradicts our assumption. Similarly, for
j^{\prime}\neq j
such that
U_{j^{\prime}}\equiv 0
, we have that
a_{j^{\prime}i}\neq 0

as
Z_{j}
and
Z_{j^{\prime}}
are not perfectly correlated. Hence the claim follows and
\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
has a Lebesgue measure of 0 on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Then we consider any
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
and show that (
B.8
) holds. Define the sets
J_{1}=\{i^{\prime}\in[d]:a_{i^{\prime}i}\leq 0\}
,
J_{1}^{\prime}=\{i^{\prime}\in[d]:a_{i^{\prime}i}\geq 0\}
,
J_{2}=\{i^{\prime}\in[d]:a_{i^{\prime}j}\leq 0\}
, and
J_{2}^{\prime}=\{i^{\prime}\in[d]:a_{i^{\prime}j}\geq 0\}
. For any
(u,v)
that is close enough to
(x,y)
, we have
(u,y)\notin\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
, and hence

\displaystyle\lim_{v\downarrow y}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}-Z_{j^{\prime}}+c_{j^{\prime}}\leq y\Big|Z_{i}=u,Z_{j}=-v+c_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle=\lim_{v\downarrow y}\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}V_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})+a_{i^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-v-\mu_{j}),&i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash i\\
U_{j^{\prime}}\leq y-c_{j^{\prime}}+\mu_{j^{\prime}}+a_{j^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-v-\mu_{j}),&j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash j\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}V_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})+a_{i^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash i,\ i^{\prime}\in J_{2}\\
U_{j^{\prime}}\leq y-c_{j^{\prime}}+\mu_{j^{\prime}}+a_{j^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash j,\ j^{\prime}\in J_{2}^{\prime}\\
V_{i^{\prime}}<x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})+a_{i^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash i,\ i^{\prime}\notin J_{2}\\
U_{j^{\prime}}<y-c_{j^{\prime}}+\mu_{j^{\prime}}+a_{j^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash j,\ j^{\prime}\notin J_{2}^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}V_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})+a_{i^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash i\\
U_{j^{\prime}}\leq y-c_{j^{\prime}}+\mu_{j^{\prime}}+a_{j^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash j\end{array}\right\},

where the last equality holds since we exclude the discontinuous points for degenerate
V_{i^{\prime}}
and
U_{j^{\prime}}
at 0 by excluding the set
\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
.

Furthermore, by similar arguments, we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{u\downarrow x}\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}V_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})+a_{i^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash i\\
U_{j^{\prime}}\leq y-c_{j^{\prime}}+\mu_{j^{\prime}}+a_{j^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash j\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}V_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(x-\mu_{i})+a_{i^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash i,\ i^{\prime}\in J_{1}\\
U_{j^{\prime}}\leq y-c_{j^{\prime}}+\mu_{j^{\prime}}+a_{j^{\prime}i}(x-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash j,\ j^{\prime}\in J_{1}^{\prime}\\
V_{i^{\prime}}<x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(x-\mu_{i})+a_{i^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash i,\ i^{\prime}\notin J_{1}\\
U_{j^{\prime}}<y-c_{j^{\prime}}+\mu_{j^{\prime}}+a_{j^{\prime}i}(x-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash j,\ j^{\prime}\notin J_{1}^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}V_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(x-\mu_{i})+a_{i^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash i\\
U_{j^{\prime}}\leq y-c_{j^{\prime}}+\mu_{j^{\prime}}+a_{j^{\prime}i}(x-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(c_{j}-y-\mu_{j}),&j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash j\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}-Z_{j^{\prime}}+c_{j^{\prime}}\leq y|Z_{i}=x,Z_{j}=-y+c_{j}\right\}.

(B.25)

Combining (
B.1.1
