) and (
B.25
), we have that (
B.8
) holds for a.e.
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})
. Hence we have that, for a.e.
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-2}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1,1})

\displaystyle=\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(i,j)\in[d]^{2}:i\neq j}\!\!\!\mathbb{P}\!\left\{\!\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}\!Z_{i^{\prime}}\!\leq\!x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\!\!-Z_{j^{\prime}}\!+\!c_{j^{\prime}}\!\leq y|Z_{i}\!=\!x,Z_{j}\!=\!-y+c_{j}\!\!\right\}\!\phi_{i,j}(x,-y+c_{j}),

and (
B.6
) follows.

Then, we show (
B.7
). For
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{p,q}
with either
p>1
or
q>1
,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{p,q})
will be upper bounded by summation of terms of the form

\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}x<Z_{i},Z_{j}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<-Z_{i^{\prime}}+c_{i^{\prime}}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right)\text{ or }\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}x<Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<-Z_{i}+c_{i},-Z_{j}+c_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right),\quad i\neq j.

We will discuss
\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}x<Z_{i},Z_{j}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<-Z_{i^{\prime}}+c_{i^{\prime}}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right)
for any
i,i^{\prime},j\in[d]
and
i\neq j
, and the results for
\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}x<Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<-Z_{i}+c_{i},-Z_{j}+c_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right)
follow with similar arguments. First consider when
i^{\prime}\notin\{i,j\}
, and we discuss the scenarios where
(Z_{i},Z_{j},-Z_{i^{\prime}}+c_{i^{\prime}})
is degenerate and non-degenerate respectively. When
(Z_{i},Z_{j},-Z_{i^{\prime}}+c_{i^{\prime}})
is non-degenerate, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}x<Z_{i},Z_{j}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<-Z_{i^{\prime}}+c_{i^{\prime}}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{x}^{x+\varepsilon}\int_{x}^{x+\varepsilon}\int_{-y-\varepsilon+c_{i^{\prime}}}^{-y+c_{i^{\prime}}}\phi_{i,j,i^{\prime}}(u,v,u^{\prime})dudvdu^{\prime}

\displaystyle=O(\varepsilon^{3})=o(\varepsilon^{2}),\quad\text{for all $(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}$ as $\varepsilon\downarrow 0$},

where
\phi_{i,j,i^{\prime}}(\cdot)
denotes the joint density of
(Z_{i},Z_{j},Z_{i^{\prime}})
, and the second equality is due to the bound
\sup_{(u,v,u^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\phi_{i,j,i^{\prime}}(u,v,u^{\prime})<+\infty
.
When
(Z_{i},Z_{j},-Z_{i^{\prime}}+c_{i^{\prime}})
is degenerate, there exists a non-zero vector
(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4})\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
such that with probability 1, we have

(Z_{i},Z_{j},-Z_{i^{\prime}}+c_{i^{\prime}})\in\mathcal{P}:=\{(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:a_{1}\cdot v_{1}+a_{2}\cdot v_{2}+a_{3}\cdot v_{3}=a_{4}\},

where
a_{3}\neq 0
by the assumption that
|\operatorname{Corr}(Z_{i},Z_{j})|<1
for any
i\neq j
. Then define the line
\mathcal{C}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:(a_{1}+a_{2})\cdot x+a_{3}\cdot y=a_{4}\}
, and for any
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\mathcal{C}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}x<Z_{i},Z_{j}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<-Z_{i^{\prime}}+c_{i^{\prime}}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\!\left(\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}x<Z_{i},Z_{j}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<-Z_{i^{\prime}}+c_{i^{\prime}}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right\}\cap\big\{\!(Z_{i},Z_{j},-Z_{i^{\prime}}+c_{i^{\prime}})\!\in\mathcal{P}\big\}\!\!\right)

\displaystyle=0,\quad\text{for small enough $\varepsilon>0$}.

Next we consider when
i^{\prime}\in\{i,j\}
, and we assume without loss of generality that
i=i^{\prime}
. Because
(x,y)\notin\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k}
, for small enough
\varepsilon>0
, we have that

\left\{(v_{1},v_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:x<v_{1}\leq x+\varepsilon,\quad y<v_{2}\leq y+\varepsilon\right\}\cap\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k}=\varnothing.

Then, since
(Z_{i},-Z_{i}+c_{i})\in\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k}
with probability 1, we have that

\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}x<Z_{i},Z_{j}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<-Z_{i^{\prime}}+c_{i^{\prime}}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(x<Z_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,y<-Z_{i}+c_{i}\leq y+\varepsilon\right)=0,

when
\varepsilon>0
is small enough. This completes the proof of (
B.7
).

Combining (
B.6
) and (
B.7
), for a.e.
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash(\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})
, we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-2}\mathbb{P}(x<M_{\mathcal{A}}\leq x+\varepsilon,y<M_{\mathcal{B}}\leq y+\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-2}\sum_{p=1}^{d}\sum_{q=1}^{d}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{p,q})=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-2}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1,1})=\widetilde{f}(x,y),

and (
B.5
) follows.
∎

B.1.2
Distribution on
\cup_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k}

.

Now we consider the distribution of
(M_{\mathcal{A}},M_{\mathcal{B}})
on the line
\mathcal{L}_{k}
for each
k=1,\ldots,d
. For any Borel measurable subset
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{R}
, we have that
\mathbb{P}(M_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{I},M_{\mathcal{B}}=-M_{\mathcal{A}}+c_{k})\leq\mathbb{P}(M_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{I})\leq\sum_{i}\mathbb{P}(Z_{i}\in\mathcal{I})
, and hence the absolute continuity holds.

Now for any given
k\in[d]
and any
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we consider the disjoint decomposition

\{x<M_{\mathcal{A}}\leq x+\varepsilon,M_{\mathcal{B}}=-M_{\mathcal{A}}+c_{k}\}=\cup_{p=1}^{d}\cup_{q=1}^{d}\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,k,x}^{p,q},

(B.26)

where the event
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,k,x}^{p,q}
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,k,x}^{p,q}

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\exists I_{1}\subseteq[d],|I_{1}|=p\\
\exists I_{2}\subseteq[d],|I_{2}|=q\end{array}:\begin{array}[]{ll}x<Z_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,&\,\,\forall i\in I_{1}\\
Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\notin I_{1}\\
-(x+\varepsilon)+c_{k}\leq-Z_{j}+c_{j}<-x+c_{k},&\,\,\forall j\in I_{2}\\
-Z_{j^{\prime}}+c_{j^{\prime}}<-(x+\varepsilon)+c_{k},&\,\,\forall j^{\prime}\notin I_{2}\\
M_{\mathcal{B}}=-M_{\mathcal{A}}+c_{k}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\exists I_{1}\subseteq[d],|I_{1}|=p\\
\exists I_{2}\subseteq[d],|I_{2}|=q\end{array}:\begin{array}[]{ll}x<Z_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,&\,\,\forall i\in I_{1}\\
Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\notin I_{1}\\
x+c_{j}-c_{k}<Z_{j}\leq x+\varepsilon+c_{j}-c_{k},&\,\,\forall j\in I_{2}\\
Z_{j^{\prime}}>x+\varepsilon+c_{j^{\prime}}-c_{k},&\,\,\forall j^{\prime}\notin I_{2}\\
M_{\mathcal{B}}=-M_{\mathcal{A}}+c_{k}\end{array}\right\}.

Recall the set
\mathcal{I}^{*}=\mathop{\text{\rm arg\,max}}_{i\in[d]}c_{i}
,
\bar{c}=\max_{i\in[d]}c_{i}
and the line
\mathcal{L}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:y=-x+\bar{c}\}=\cup_{k\in\mathcal{I}^{*}}\mathcal{L}_{k}
. We first show that for
k\notin\mathcal{I}^{*}
,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,k,x}^{p,q})=0
for any
p,q
when
\varepsilon>0
is small enough.
Suppose that
k\notin\mathcal{I}^{*}
. Then, there exists an
i\in[d]
such that
c_{i}>c_{k}
. If
i\notin I_{2}
, we have
x+\varepsilon+c_{i}-c_{k}>x+\varepsilon
, and if
i\in I_{2}
, we have that
x+c_{i}-c_{k}>x+\varepsilon
for small enough
\varepsilon>0
. Hence when
\varepsilon
is small enough, we have that the conditions
Z_{i}>x+\varepsilon
and
Z_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon
cannot hold simultaneously. This implies that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,k,x}^{p,q})=0
for any
p,q
. Therefore, combining with the decomposition (
B.26
), for any
k\notin\mathcal{I}^{*}
,
\mathbb{P}\{x<M_{\mathcal{A}}\leq x+\varepsilon,M_{\mathcal{B}}=-M_{\mathcal{A}}+c_{k}\}=0
when
\varepsilon
is small enough. This also implies that
(M_{\mathcal{A}},M_{\mathcal{B}})
is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
on
\mathcal{L}_{k}
for
k\notin\mathcal{I}^{*}
, and we have that result (1) of Lemma
B.1
follows.

Finally, we consider the line
\mathcal{L}=\cup_{k\in\mathcal{I}^{*}}\mathcal{L}_{k}
. For any given
k\in\mathcal{I}^{*}
(note that
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,k,x}^{p,q}
denotes the same set for any
k\in\mathcal{I}^{*}
) and any
p,q\geq 1
, if there exists
i\neq j
such that
i,j\in I_{1}\cup I_{2}
, we have that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,k,x}^{p,q})=O(\varepsilon^{2})
as
(Z_{i},Z_{j})
is non-degenerate. Similarly, we have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,k,x}^{p,q})=O(\varepsilon^{2})=o(\varepsilon)
for either
p>1
or
q>1
. Therefore, we can write

\mathbb{P}(\cup_{p=1}^{d}\cup_{q=1}^{d}\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,k,x}^{p,q})=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,k,x}^{1,1})+o(\varepsilon)=\sum_{i\in[d]}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x}^{(i)})+o(\varepsilon),

where

\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x}^{(i)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}c_{i}=\bar{c},&\\
x<Z_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,&\\
x+\varepsilon+c_{i^{\prime}}-\bar{c}<Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\neq i\end{array}\right\}.

For each
i\in[d]
, it can be seen that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x}^{(i)})=0
if
i\notin\mathcal{I}^{*}
.
Furthermore, if
|\mathcal{I}^{*}|>1
, for each
i\in[d]
, there exists
i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}^{*}
and
i^{\prime}\neq i
such that
x+\varepsilon+c_{i^{\prime}}-\bar{c}=x+\varepsilon\geq x
, and
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x}^{(i)})=0
. Hence, we have that the density is 0 for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
if
|\mathcal{I}^{*}|>1
.

We now consider
|\mathcal{I}^{*}|=1
and
\mathcal{I}^{*}=\{i^{*}\}
for some
i^{*}\in[d]
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x}^{(i^{*})})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}x<Z_{i^{*}}\leq x+\varepsilon,&\\
x+\varepsilon+c_{i}-\bar{c}<Z_{i}\leq x,&\,\,\forall i\neq i^{*}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle=\int_{x}^{x+\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}\left(x+\varepsilon+c_{i}-\bar{c}<Z_{i}\leq x,\,\forall i\neq i^{*}|Z_{i^{*}}=u\right)\phi_{i^{*}}(u)du

\displaystyle=\int_{x}^{x+\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}\left(x+c_{i}-\bar{c}\leq Z_{i}\leq x,\,\forall i\neq i^{*}|Z_{i^{*}}=u\right)\phi_{i^{*}}(u)du

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{x}^{x+\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}\left(x+c_{i}-\bar{c}\leq Z_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon+c_{i}-\bar{c},\,\forall i\neq i^{*}|Z_{i^{*}}=u\right)\phi_{i^{*}}(u)du

\displaystyle=\int_{x}^{x+\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}\left(x+c_{i}-\bar{c}\leq Z_{i}\leq x,\,\forall i\neq i^{*}|Z_{i^{*}}=u\right)\phi_{i^{*}}(u)du+O(\varepsilon^{2}),

where the
O(\varepsilon^{2})
term in the last equality follows as
(Z_{i},Z_{i^{*}})
is non-degenerate for any
i\neq i^{*}
, which also gives

\lim_{u\downarrow x}\mathbb{P}\left(x+c_{i}-\bar{c}\leq Z_{i}\leq x,\,\forall i\neq i^{*}|Z_{i^{*}}=u\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(x+c_{i}-\bar{c}\leq Z_{i}\leq x,\,\forall i\neq i^{*}|Z_{i^{*}}=x\right).

Hence for
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}\{x<M_{\mathcal{A}}\leq x+\varepsilon,M_{\mathcal{B}}=-M_{\mathcal{A}}+c_{k}\}=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x}^{(i^{*})})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(x+c_{i}-\bar{c}\leq Z_{i}\leq x,\,\forall i\neq i^{*}|Z_{i^{*}}=x\right)\phi_{i^{*}}(x),

and result (2) of Lemma
B.1
holds. ∎

∎

B.2
Proof of Lemma
B.2

.

Recall that we define
\bar{c}=\max_{i}c_{i}
, the set
\mathcal{I}^{*}=\mathop{\text{\rm arg\,max}}_{i}c_{i}
and the line
\mathcal{L}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:y=-x+\bar{c}\}
. By Lemma
B.1
, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}}-t|\leq\varepsilon)=\int_{(x,y)\in\mathcal{L}^{c}:|y-x-t|\leq\varepsilon}\widetilde{f}(x,y)dxdy+\int_{|-2x+\bar{c}-t|\leq\varepsilon}\widetilde{f}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{|y-x-t|\leq\varepsilon}\widetilde{f}(x,y)dxdy+\int_{-\frac{t-\bar{c}}{2}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{-\frac{t-\bar{c}}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\widetilde{f}(x)dx

\displaystyle\overset{u=y-x}{=\joinrel=}\underbrace{\int_{|u-t|\leq\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{f}(x,x+u)dxdu}_{={\rm I_{1}}}+\underbrace{\int_{-\frac{t-\bar{c}}{2}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{-\frac{t-\bar{c}}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\widetilde{f}(x)dx}_{={\rm I_{2}}},

(B.27)

where the second equality follows from the fact that
\mathcal{L}
has measure 0 on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and
\widetilde{f}(x,y)
is finite everywhere. We consider the terms
{\rm I_{1}}
and
{\rm I_{2}}
independently. For
{\rm I_{1}}
, we have that

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{1}\!

\displaystyle=\!\int_{t-\varepsilon}^{t+\varepsilon}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{(i,j)\in[d]^{2}:i\neq j}\bigg\{\mathbb{P}\!\left(\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x\\
\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}(-Z_{j^{\prime}}+c_{j^{\prime}})\leq x+u\end{array}\!\bigg|\!\begin{array}[]{c}Z_{i}=x\\
Z_{j}=-x-u+c_{j}\end{array}\!\!\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\phi_{i,j}(x,-x-u+c_{j})\bigg\}dxdu

\displaystyle=\int_{t-\varepsilon}^{t+\varepsilon}\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{i\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x\\
\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime}}\leq x\end{array}\bigg|\begin{array}[]{c}Z_{i}=x\\
\widetilde{Z}_{j}=x\end{array}\right)\phi_{Z_{i},\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(x,x)dxdu

\displaystyle=\int_{t-\varepsilon}^{t+\varepsilon}\sum_{j\in[d]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{i\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x\\
\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime}}\leq x\end{array}\bigg|\begin{array}[]{c}Z_{i}=x\\
\widetilde{Z}_{j}=x\end{array}\right)\phi_{Z_{i}|\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(x|x)\phi_{\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(x)dxdu,

where
\widetilde{Z}_{j}=-Z_{j}+c_{j}-u
for
j\in[d]
;
\phi_{Z_{i},\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(\cdot)
denotes the joint density of
(Z_{i},\widetilde{Z}_{j})
;
\phi_{\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(\cdot)
denotes the marginal density of
\widetilde{Z}_{j}
;
\phi_{Z_{i}|\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(\cdot)
denotes the conditional density of
Z_{i}
on
\widetilde{Z}_{j}
, and the second equality follows from

\phi_{Z_{i},\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(x,x)=\phi_{i,j}(x,-x-u+c_{j}).

Then, we let

\displaystyle g_{u,j}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x\\
\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime}}\leq x\end{array}\bigg|\begin{array}[]{c}Z_{i}=x\\
\widetilde{Z}_{j}=x\end{array}\right)\phi_{Z_{i}|\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(x|x),

\displaystyle H_{u,j}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\max_{i\in[d]}Z_{i}\leq x\\
\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime}}\leq x\end{array}\bigg|\widetilde{Z}_{j}=x\right),

and we have the following claim for
g_{u,j}(x)
and
H_{u,j}(x)
.

Claim B.1
.

For every
j\in\mathcal{B}
and a.e.
u\in\mathbb{R}
, we have that

g_{u,j}(x)=0,\quad\forall x<(c_{j}-u)/2,

(B.28)

and

g_{u,j}(x)\leq(1+\underline{\rho})^{-1}dH_{u,j}(x)/dx,\quad\forall x>(c_{j}-u)/2,

(B.29)

where
\underline{\rho}=\min_{i\neq j}\rho_{ij}
with
\rho_{ij}=\operatorname{Corr}(Z_{i},Z_{j})=\sigma^{-2}{\rm Cov}(Z_{i},Z_{j})
.

Proof.

See Appendix
C.5
.
∎

Following Claim
B.1
, together with the fact that

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\!\sum_{(i,j)\in[d]^{2}:i\neq j}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{P}\!\!\left(\!\begin{array}[]{c}\displaystyle\max_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{i\}}Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x\\
\displaystyle\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\!\!-Z_{j^{\prime}}+c_{j^{\prime}}\leq x+u\end{array}\Bigg|\!\begin{array}[]{c}Z_{i}=x\\
Z_{j}\!=\!-x\!-\!u\!+\!c_{j}\end{array}\!\!\right)\!\!\phi_{i,j}(x,-x\!-\!u\!+\!c_{j})dx\right|\!\!<\!\infty,

we have

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{t-\varepsilon}^{t+\varepsilon}\sum_{j\in[d]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}g_{u,j}(x)\phi_{\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(x)dxdu

\displaystyle\overset{\rm(a)}{=}\int_{t-\varepsilon}^{t+\varepsilon}\sum_{j\in[d]}\int_{(c_{j}-u)/2}^{+\infty}g_{u,j}(x)\phi_{\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(x)dxdu

\displaystyle\overset{\rm(b)}{\leq}\frac{1}{1+\underline{\rho}}\int_{t-\varepsilon}^{t+\varepsilon}\sum_{j\in[d]}\int_{(c_{j}-u)/2}^{+\infty}\phi_{\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(x)dH_{u,j}(x)du

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+\underline{\rho}}\!\int_{t-\varepsilon}^{t+\varepsilon}\sum_{j\in[d]}\Big\{\phi_{\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(x)H_{u,j}(x)\Big|_{\frac{c_{j}-u}{2}}^{+\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\!\!\int_{(c_{j}-u)/2}^{+\infty}\!\!H_{u,j}(x)\phi_{\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(x)\frac{x+\mu_{j}-c_{j}+u}{\sigma^{2}}dx\!\Big\}\!du

\displaystyle\overset{\rm(c)}{\leq}\frac{1}{(1+\underline{\rho})}\!\!\int_{t-\varepsilon}^{t+\varepsilon}\!\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}}\!\!\Big\{\!\!\sum_{j\in[d]}\!\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\!\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime}}\!\leq\!x\Big|\widetilde{Z}_{j}\!=\!x\!\Big)\!\frac{|x+\mu_{j}-c_{j}+u|}{\sigma^{2}}\phi_{\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}\!(x)\!\!\Big\}dxdu

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1+\underline{\rho})\sigma^{2}}\int_{t-\varepsilon}^{t+\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{I}\Big\{\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime}}\leq\widetilde{Z}_{j}\Big\}|\widetilde{Z}_{j}-\widetilde{\mu}_{j}|\Big)du

\displaystyle\overset{\rm(d)}{\leq}\frac{1}{(1+\underline{\rho})\sigma^{2}}\int_{t-\varepsilon}^{t+\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}\big(\max_{j\in[d]}|\widetilde{Z}_{j}-\widetilde{\mu}_{j}|\big)du

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\big(\max_{i\in[d]}|Z_{i}-\mu_{i}|\big)\frac{\varepsilon}{(1+\underline{\rho})\sigma^{2}},

(B.30)

where
\widetilde{\mu}_{j}=\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{Z}_{j})=-\mu_{j}+c_{j}-u
; equality (a) follows from (
B.28
); inequality (b) holds because of (
B.29
);
inequality (c) follows from
H_{u,j}(x)\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\max_{j^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime}}\leq x\big|\widetilde{Z}_{j}=x\!\big)
and the fact that

\phi_{\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(x)H_{u,j}(x)\Big|_{(c_{j}-u)/2}^{+\infty}\leq 0,\quad\forall j\in[d],

and inequality (d) follows by the assumption
|\operatorname{Corr}(Z_{j},Z_{j^{\prime}})|<1
for any
j^{\prime}\neq j
such that
\mathbb{P}(\max_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime}}=\widetilde{Z}_{j})=0
for any
j\in[d]
.

For term
{\rm I}_{2}
, by Lemma
B.1
, when
|\mathcal{I}^{*}|>1
, we have
{\rm I}_{2}=0
; When
\mathcal{I}^{*}=\{i^{*}\}
for some
i^{*}\in[d]
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\frac{t-\bar{c}}{2}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{-\frac{t-\bar{c}}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\mathbb{P}\big(x+c_{i}-\bar{c}\leq Z_{i}\leq x,\forall i\neq i^{*}|Z_{i^{*}}=x\big)\phi_{i^{*}}(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-\frac{t-\bar{c}}{2}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{-\frac{t-\bar{c}}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\phi_{i^{*}}(x)dx\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\underline{\rho})^{-1}\mathbb{E}\big(\max_{i}|Z_{i}-\mu_{i}|\big)\varepsilon/\sigma^{2},

(B.31)

where the second inequality holds by the bound on the Gaussian density function, and the last inequality holds by the fact that

(1+\underline{\rho})^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\max_{i}|Z_{i}-\mu_{i}|/\sigma]\geq(1+\underline{\rho})^{-1}\mathbb{E}[|Z_{1}-\mu_{1}|/\sigma]=(1+\underline{\rho})^{-1}\sqrt{2/\pi}\geq 1/\sqrt{2\pi}.

Then combining (
B.27
), (
B.30
) and (
B.31
), for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have that

\mathbb{P}(|M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}}-t|\leq\varepsilon)\leq 3(1+\underline{\rho})^{-1}\mathbb{E}\big(\max_{i}|Z_{i}-\mu_{i}|\big)\varepsilon/\sigma^{2},

and (
B.3
) holds as desired.

∎

B.3
Proof of Lemma
B.3

.

We first show that
|{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}|=|{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}|
.
For any
i\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}
, by the definitions of
{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}
in (
5.1
), there exists some
j\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}
such that
\operatorname{Corr}(X_{i},X_{j})=-1
. Now assume that there also exists some other
j^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}},j^{\prime}\neq j
such that
\operatorname{Corr}(X_{i},X_{j^{\prime}})=-1
, then we have that
\operatorname{Corr}(X_{j},X_{j^{\prime}})=1
, which contradicts our assumption that
\operatorname{Corr}(X_{j},X_{j^{\prime}})<1
for all
j,j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}
and
j\neq j^{\prime}
. Hence, for any
i\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}
,
X_{i}
is perfectly negatively correlated with one and only one
X_{j}
with
j\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}
. By the same argument, for any
j\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}
,
X_{j}
is perfectly negatively correlated with one and only one
X_{i}
with
i\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}
, and hence we have
|{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}|=|{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}|
. Besides, by the definition of
{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}
, there exist constants
c_{i}
’s such that
\{X_{j}\}_{j\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\{-X_{i}+c_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}}
, and (
B.4
) holds.

Meanwhile, for any
i,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
i\neq i^{\prime}
, suppose that
\operatorname{Corr}(X_{i},X_{i^{\prime}})=-1
. Then by our assumption, there exists some
j\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}
such that
\operatorname{Corr}(X_{i},X_{j})=-1
, and we have that
\operatorname{Corr}(X_{i^{\prime}},X_{j})=1
, which contradicts our assumption that
\operatorname{Corr}(X_{i},X_{j})<1
for any
i\neq j
. Hence we have that
|\operatorname{Corr}(X_{i},X_{i^{\prime}})|<1
for any
i,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
i\neq i^{\prime}
. The same result holds for
{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathcal{B}}
by the same argument, which completes our proof.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs of Claims and Auxiliary Lemmas in Appendix

C.1
Proofs of claims for Lemma
2.5

C.1.1
Proof of Claim
A.1

.

We first
decompose
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1,1}
into disjoint events as

\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1,1}=\cup_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\cup_{j\in\mathcal{B}}\Big\{\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}}X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y,x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,y<X_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon\Big\},

and we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1,1})=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},j\in\mathcal{B}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}}X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y,x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,y<X_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\quad=\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A},j\in\mathcal{B}}\int_{x}^{x+\varepsilon}\!\!\!\int_{y}^{y+\varepsilon}\!\!\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}}X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=u,X_{j}=v\Big)\phi_{i,j}(u,v)dudv,

where the last equality holds by the assumption that
(X_{i},X_{j})
are non-degenerate for any
i\in\mathcal{A}
and
j\in\mathcal{B}
, and
\phi_{i,j}(u,v)
is the joint density at
(X_{i},X_{j})=(u,v)
.

Then, we prove Claim
A.1
by showing that

\displaystyle\lim_{u\downarrow x,v\downarrow y}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}}X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=u,X_{j}=v\Big)

(C.1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}}X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=x,X_{j}=y\Big),

for a.e.
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and every
i\in\mathcal{A},j\in\mathcal{B}
. Specifically, for every
i\in\mathcal{A}
and
j\in\mathcal{B}
, we construct a set
\mathcal{D}_{i,j}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that
\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
has a Lebesgue measure of 0 in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and for any
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
, (
C.1
) holds.

To construct the set
\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
, for
i^{\prime}\in[p]\backslash\{i,j\}
, we first define the residual from orthogonal projection of
X_{i^{\prime}}
onto
(X_{i},X_{j})
as

U_{i^{\prime}}=X_{i^{\prime}}-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(X_{i}-\mu_{i})-a_{i^{\prime}j}(X_{j}-\mu_{j}),

where
(a_{i^{\prime}i},a_{i^{\prime}j})=\Sigma_{i^{\prime},ij}\Sigma_{ij}^{-1}

with
\Sigma_{ij}=\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-\mu_{i},X_{j}-\mu_{j})^{\top}(X_{i}-\mu_{i},X_{j}-\mu_{j})]
and
\Sigma_{i^{\prime},ij}=\mathbb{E}[(X_{i^{\prime}}-\mu_{i^{\prime}})(X_{i}-\mu_{i},X_{j}-\mu_{j})]
. We have that
U_{i^{\prime}}
is independent of
(X_{i},X_{j})
.
Then, we consider the
U_{i^{\prime}}
’s that are degenerate, i.e.,
U_{i^{\prime}}\equiv 0
, and we define
\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
as

\mathcal{D}_{i,j}=\displaystyle\bigcup_{i^{\prime}\notin\{i,j\}:U_{i^{\prime}}\equiv 0}\!\!\big\{\!(x,y)\!\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:(\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}\{i^{\prime}\}-a_{i^{\prime}i})\cdot x+(\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}\{i^{\prime}\}-a_{i^{\prime}j})\cdot y=\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}\mu_{i}-a_{i^{\prime}j}\mu_{j}\big\},

where
\mathcal{D}_{i,j}=\varnothing
if
U_{i^{\prime}}\not\equiv 0
for any
i^{\prime}\notin\{i,j\}
.
We first show that
\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
is a union of lines or empty sets and has a Lebesgue measure of 0 in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. To do so, it suffices to show that
(\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}\{i^{\prime}\}-a_{i^{\prime}i},\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}\{i^{\prime}\}-a_{i^{\prime}j})\neq\mathbf{0}
for any
i^{\prime}\in[p]\backslash\{i,j\}
such that
U_{i^{\prime}}\equiv 0
. When
U_{i^{\prime}}\equiv 0
, we have

X_{i^{\prime}}-\mu_{i^{\prime}}\equiv a_{i^{\prime}i}(X_{i}-\mu_{i})+a_{i^{\prime}j}(X_{j}-\mu_{j}).

For
(a_{i^{\prime}i},a_{i^{\prime}j})
, since
\sigma_{i^{\prime}}^{2}>0
, at least one of
a_{i^{\prime}i}
and
a_{i^{\prime}j}
is non-zero. When
a_{i^{\prime}i}a_{i^{\prime}j}\neq 0
, we have
(\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}\{i^{\prime}\}-a_{i^{\prime}i},\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}\{i^{\prime}\}-a_{i^{\prime}j})\neq\mathbf{0}
. When
a_{i^{\prime}i}a_{i^{\prime}j}=0
, since
{\rm Var}(X_{i^{\prime}}-X_{i}),{\rm Var}(X_{i^{\prime}}-X_{j})>0
, we have that either
a_{i^{\prime}i}\neq 1
or
a_{i^{\prime}j}\neq 1
, and
(\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}\{i^{\prime}\}-a_{i^{\prime}i},\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}\{i^{\prime}\}-a_{i^{\prime}j})\neq\mathbf{0}
. Hence we conclude that
\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
is a union of lines or empty sets and has a Lebesgue measure of 0 in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Next, we show that (
C.1
) holds for any
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
. Define the sets
J_{1}=\{i^{\prime}\in[p]\backslash\{i,j\}:a_{i^{\prime}j}\leq 0\}
,
J_{1}^{c}=[p]\backslash\{i,j\}\backslash J_{1}
,
J_{2}=\{i^{\prime}\in[p]\backslash\{i,j\}:a_{i^{\prime}i}\leq 0\}
, and
J_{2}^{c}=[p]\backslash\{i,j\}\backslash J_{2}
. Then we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{v\downarrow y}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}}X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=u,X_{j}=v\Big)

\displaystyle=\lim_{v\downarrow y}\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}U_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{i^{\prime}j}(v-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}\\
U_{j^{\prime}}\leq y-\mu_{j^{\prime}}-a_{j^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(v-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}\end{array}\Bigg|X_{i}=u,X_{j}=v\right\}

\displaystyle=\lim_{v\downarrow y}\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}U_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{i^{\prime}j}(v-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}\\
U_{j^{\prime}}\leq y-\mu_{j^{\prime}}-a_{j^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(v-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}U_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{i^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\})\cap J_{1}\\
U_{j^{\prime}}\leq y-\mu_{j^{\prime}}-a_{j^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall j^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\})\cap J_{1}\\
U_{i^{\prime}}<x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{i^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\})\cap J_{1}^{c}\\
U_{j^{\prime}}<y-\mu_{j^{\prime}}-a_{j^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall j^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\})\cap J_{1}^{c}\end{array}\right\}.

We note that each
U_{i^{\prime}}
either degenerates to 0 or has a non-degenerate Gaussian distribution, and the mapping is discontinuous only for degenerate
U_{i^{\prime}}
’s at
0
. When
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small, since
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
, we have

\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}\{i^{\prime}\}\cdot x+\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}\{i^{\prime}\}\cdot y-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{i^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j})\neq 0,\quad\forall i^{\prime}\in[p]\backslash\{i,j\}.

Hence by continuity, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{v\downarrow y}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}}X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=u,X_{j}=v\Big)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}U_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{i^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}\\
U_{j^{\prime}}\leq y-\mu_{j^{\prime}}-a_{j^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}}X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=u,X_{j}=y\Big).

(C.4)

By similar arguments, we also have that

\displaystyle\lim_{u\downarrow x}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}}X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=u,X_{j}=y\Big)

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{u\downarrow x}\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}U_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{i^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}\\
U_{j^{\prime}}\leq y-\mu_{j^{\prime}}-a_{j^{\prime}i}(u-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}U_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(x-\mu_{i})-a_{i^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\})\cap J_{2}\\
U_{j^{\prime}}\leq y-\mu_{j^{\prime}}-a_{j^{\prime}i}(x-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall j^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\})\cap J_{2}\\
U_{i^{\prime}}<x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(x-\mu_{i})-a_{i^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\})\cap J_{2}^{c}\\
U_{j^{\prime}}<y-\mu_{j^{\prime}}-a_{j^{\prime}i}(x-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall j^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\})\cap J_{2}^{c}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}U_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-a_{i^{\prime}i}(x-\mu_{i})-a_{i^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}\\
U_{j^{\prime}}\leq y-\mu_{j^{\prime}}-a_{j^{\prime}i}(x-\mu_{i})-a_{j^{\prime}j}(y-\mu_{j}),&\,\,\forall j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}}X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=x,X_{j}=y\Big).

(C.13)

Combining (
C.4
) and (
C.13
), we have that (
C.1
) holds for any
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\mathcal{D}_{i,j}
, which further gives that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-2}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1,1})=\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{j\}}X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=x,X_{j}=y\Big)\phi_{i,j}(x,y),

for a.e.
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. This completes the proof.
∎

C.1.2
Proof of Claim
A.2

.

First, observe that for
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{q,k}
with either
q>1
or
k>1
,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{q,k})
is bounded by a sum of terms of the form

\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
x<X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<X_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right),\quad i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A},\quad i\neq i^{\prime},\quad j\in\mathcal{B},

\text{ or }\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<X_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon\\
y<X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right),\quad i\in\mathcal{A},\quad j,j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B},\quad j\neq j^{\prime}.

We consider
\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}x<X_{i},X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<X_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right)
for
i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
,
i\neq i^{\prime}
and
j\in\mathcal{B}
first, and we discuss the scenarios when
(X_{i},X_{i^{\prime}},X_{j})^{\top}
is degenerate and non-degenerate respectively.

When
(X_{i},X_{i^{\prime}},X_{j})^{\top}
is non-degenerate, we have that

\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
x<X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<X_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right)=O(\varepsilon^{3})=o(\varepsilon^{2}),\quad i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A},\quad i\neq i^{\prime},\quad j\in\mathcal{B},

(C.14)

as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
for any
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

When
(X_{i},X_{i^{\prime}},X_{j})^{\top}
is degenerate, we have that there exists a non-zero vector
(c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4})
such that
c_{1}\cdot X_{i}+c_{2}\cdot X_{i^{\prime}}+c_{3}\cdot X_{j}\equiv c_{4}
, and we define the two planes

\mathcal{P}^{1}=\{(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:v_{1}=v_{2}\},\,\mathcal{P}^{2}=\{(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:c_{1}\cdot v_{1}+c_{2}\cdot v_{2}+c_{3}\cdot v_{3}=c_{4}\},

and their intersection is

\mathcal{P}^{1}\cap\mathcal{P}^{2}=\{(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:(c_{1}+c_{2})v_{1}+c_{3}v_{3}=(c_{1}+c_{2})v_{2}+c_{3}v_{3}=c_{4}\}.

Then, we have that
(x,x,y)\in\mathcal{P}^{1}
and
(X_{i},X_{i^{\prime}},X_{j})^{\top}\in\mathcal{P}^{2}
with probability 1, and for any
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that
(c_{1}+c_{2})x+c_{3}y\neq c_{4}
, the point
(x,x,y)
does not fall onto the plane
\mathcal{P}^{2}
. Then, when
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small, we have that

\{(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:x<v_{1}\leq x+\varepsilon,x<v_{2}\leq x+\varepsilon,y<v_{3}\leq y+\varepsilon\}\cap\mathcal{P}^{2}=\varnothing,

which implies

\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
x<X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<X_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right)=0,\quad i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A},\quad i\neq i^{\prime},\quad j\in\mathcal{B}.

(C.15)

Hence it suffices to show that
(c_{1}+c_{2},c_{3})\neq\mathbf{0}
so that
(c_{1}+c_{2})x+c_{3}y=c_{4}
occurs with a Lebesgue measure of 0 on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. When
c_{3}\neq 0
, we have
(c_{1}+c_{2},c_{3})\neq\mathbf{0}
.
When
c_{3}=0
, since
(c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4})
is non-zero,
(c_{1},c_{2})
is non-zero, because otherwise,
c_{4}=c_{1}=c_{2}=c_{3}=0
. If
c_{1}+c_{2}=0
, then we have that
{\rm Var}(X_{i}-X_{i^{\prime}})=0
, which contradicts our assumption that
{\rm Var}(X_{i}-X_{j})>0
for any
i\neq j
. Hence we have that
c_{1}+c_{2}\neq 0
, and
(c_{1}+c_{2},c_{3})\neq\mathbf{0}
.

Therefore, combining (
C.14
) and (
C.15
), for a.e.
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we have that

\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
x<X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<X_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right)=o(\epsilon^{2}),\quad\text{for any }i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A},\,i\neq i^{\prime}\text{ and }j\in\mathcal{B}.

Following similar arguments, we have that
\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon\\
y<X_{j}\leq y+\varepsilon\\
y<X_{j^{\prime}}\leq y+\varepsilon\end{array}\right)=o(\varepsilon^{2})
for any
i\in\mathcal{A}
,
j,j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}
,
j\neq j^{\prime}
. Hence
we have that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{q,k})=o(\varepsilon^{2})
when
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
for a.e.
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
when
q>1
or
k>1
, which completes the proof.
∎

C.1.3
Proof of Claim
A.3

.

We first decompose
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1}
into disjoint events,

\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1}=\bigcup_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\Big\{\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}X_{j}\leq y,x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon\Big\},

and we have

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1})=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\int_{x}^{x+\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}X_{j}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=u\Big)\phi_{i}(u)du.

(C.16)

We show that the mapping
u\mapsto\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}X_{j}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=u\Big)
is right continuous at
x
for all
i\in\mathcal{A}
and a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Specifically, given
i\in\mathcal{A}
, for every
i^{\prime}\in[p]\backslash\{i\}
, define the orthogonal residual
V_{i^{\prime}}=X_{i^{\prime}}-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-\sigma_{i}^{-2}\sigma_{ii^{\prime}}(X_{i}-\mu_{i})
, where
\sigma_{ii^{\prime}}=\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}-\mu_{i})(X_{i^{\prime}}-\mu_{i^{\prime}})]
. Then, define the set

\mathcal{D}_{i}=\cup_{i^{\prime}\in[p]\backslash\{i\}:V_{i^{\prime}}\equiv 0}\big\{x\in\mathbb{R}:(\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(i^{\prime})-\sigma_{i}^{-2}\sigma_{ii^{\prime}})\cdot x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}+\sigma_{i}^{-2}\sigma_{ii^{\prime}}\cdot\mu_{i}+\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}(i^{\prime})\cdot y=0\big\},

where
\mathcal{D}_{i}=\varnothing
if there is no
i^{\prime}\in[p]\backslash\{i\}
such that
V_{i^{\prime}}\equiv 0
. We show that
\mathcal{D}_{i}
has a Lebesgue measure of 0 on
\mathbb{R}
, and for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\mathcal{D}_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{u\downarrow x}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}X_{j}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=u\Big)

(C.17)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}X_{j}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=x\Big).

We first show that
\mathcal{D}_{i}
is a union of points or empty sets. Indeed, for
i^{\prime}\in[p]\backslash\{i\}
, if
V_{i^{\prime}}\equiv 0
, i.e.,
(X_{i^{\prime}},X_{i})
is degenerate, we have
\sigma_{i}^{-2}\sigma_{ii^{\prime}}\neq 0
. Meanwhile, for
i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
such that
V_{i^{\prime}}\equiv 0
, if
\sigma_{i}^{-2}\sigma_{ii^{\prime}}=1
, we have
\mu_{i^{\prime}}\neq\mu_{i}
, since otherwise, we have
X_{i^{\prime}}\equiv X_{i}
, which contradicts our assumption that
X_{i^{\prime}}\not\equiv X_{i}
for any
i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
and
i\neq i^{\prime}
. Hence, we have that
\mathcal{D}_{i}
is a properly defined union of points or empty sets with a Lebesgue measure of 0 on
\mathbb{R}
.

Next, we show that (
C.17
) holds for any
x\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\mathcal{D}_{i}
. Define the sets
J=\{i^{\prime}\in[p]\backslash\{i\}:\sigma_{ii^{\prime}}\leq 0\}
and
J^{c}=[p]\backslash\{i\}\backslash J
. Then for any
x\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\mathcal{D}_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{u\downarrow x}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}X_{j}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=u\Big)

\displaystyle=\lim_{u\downarrow x}\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}V_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-\sigma_{i}^{-2}\sigma_{ii^{\prime}}(u-\mu_{i}),&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}\\
V_{j}\leq y-\mu_{j}-\sigma_{i}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{i}),&\,\,\forall j\in\mathcal{B}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}V_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-\sigma_{i}^{-2}\sigma_{ii^{\prime}}(x-\mu_{i}),&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\})\cap J\\
V_{j}\leq y-\mu_{j}-\sigma_{i}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(x-\mu_{i}),&\,\,\forall j\in\mathcal{B}\cap J\\
V_{i^{\prime}}<x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-\sigma_{i}^{-2}\sigma_{ii^{\prime}}(x-\mu_{i}),&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\})\cap J^{c}\\
V_{j}<y-\mu_{j}-\sigma_{i}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(x-\mu_{i}),&\,\,\forall j\in\mathcal{B}\cap J^{c}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}V_{i^{\prime}}\leq x-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-\sigma_{i}^{-2}\sigma_{ii^{\prime}}(x-\mu_{i}),&\,\,\forall i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}\\
V_{j}\leq y-\mu_{j}-\sigma_{i}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(x-\mu_{i}),&\,\,\forall j\in\mathcal{B}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}X_{j}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=x\Big),

and (
C.17
) holds.

Combining (
C.16
) and (
C.17
), for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-1}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{1})=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\backslash\{i\}}X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}}X_{j}\leq y\Big|X_{i}=x\Big)\phi_{i}(x),

which completes the proof.
∎

C.1.4
Proof of Claim
A.4

.

We show that for
k\geq 2
,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{k})=o(\varepsilon)
as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}
. When
|\mathcal{A}|=1
, the results hold trivially. When
|\mathcal{A}|>1
, we have that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,x,y}^{k})
is upper bounded by a sum of terms of the form
\mathbb{P}(x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,x<X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+\varepsilon)
, where
i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
and
i\neq i^{\prime}
. When
(X_{i},X_{i^{\prime}})
is non-degenerate, we have
\mathbb{P}(x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,x<X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+\varepsilon)=O(\varepsilon^{2})=o(\varepsilon)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
, and Claim
A.4
holds. When
(X_{i},X_{i^{\prime}})
is degenerate, there exists a non-zero vector
(c_{1},c_{2},c_{3})
such that
c_{1}\cdot X_{i}+c_{2}\cdot X_{i^{\prime}}=c_{3}
. Note that
(c_{1},c_{2})\neq\mathbf{0}
, and
(c_{1}+c_{2})
and
c_{3}
cannot both be 0, since otherwise, we have
X_{i}\equiv X_{i^{\prime}}
, which contradicts our assumption. Hence the set
\mathcal{C}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}:(c_{1}+c_{2})\cdot x=c_{3}\}
is either a single point or an empty set with a Lebesgue measure of 0 on
\mathbb{R}
. When
x\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\mathcal{C}
, and
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small, we have

\{(v_{1},v_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:x<v_{1}\leq x+\varepsilon,x<v_{2}\leq x+\varepsilon\}\cap\{(v_{1},v_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:c_{1}\cdot v_{1}+c_{2}\cdot v_{2}=c_{3}\}=\varnothing,

and
\mathbb{P}(x<X_{i}\leq x+\varepsilon,x<X_{i^{\prime}}\leq x+\varepsilon)=0
. Hence, Claim
A.4
follows.
∎

C.2
Proof of Claim
A.5
for Lemma
2.6

.

We begin with
i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
and
i\neq i^{\prime}
, and we show that (
A.4
) holds for a.e.
u\in\mathbb{R}
, i.e.,

\frac{V_{i}+\mu_{i}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j})}{1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}}\not\equiv\frac{V_{i^{\prime}}+\mu_{i^{\prime}}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j}(u-\mu_{j})}{1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j}},\quad\text{ $\forall i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}$ and $i\neq i^{\prime}$}.

(C.18)

Assume that the two random variables on the left-hand side (LHS) and RHS of (
C.18
) are identical, i.e.,
\widetilde{V}_{i}\equiv\widetilde{V}_{i^{\prime}}
. Then, since
\mathbb{E}V_{i}=\mathbb{E}V_{i^{\prime}}=0
, we have that

\displaystyle(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}V_{i}=(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\left(X_{i}-\mu_{i}-\sigma_{j}^{-1}\sigma_{ij}(X_{j}-\mu_{j})\right)

\displaystyle\equiv(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j})^{-1}\left(X_{i^{\prime}}-\mu_{i^{\prime}}-\sigma_{j}^{-1}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j}(X_{j}-\mu_{j})\right)=(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j})^{-1}V_{i^{\prime}}.

Then, if

(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}=(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j})^{-1}\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j},

by some algebra, we have that
(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}=(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j})^{-1}
, and hence
X_{i}-\mu_{i}\equiv X_{i^{\prime}}-\mu_{i^{\prime}}
, which contradicts the assumption that
{\rm Var}(X_{i}-X_{i^{\prime}})>0
. Hence,

(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij})^{-1}\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}\neq(1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j})^{-1}\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j},

which indicates that
\widetilde{V}_{i}\equiv\widetilde{V}_{i^{\prime}}
only holds at

u=\left(\frac{\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}}{1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}}-\frac{\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j}}{1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j}}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\mu_{i^{\prime}}}{1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j}}-\frac{\mu_{i}}{1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}}\right)+\mu_{j}.

Therefore, (
C.18
) and equivalently (
A.4
) holds for a.e.
u\in\mathbb{R}
.

Secondly, for
i\in\mathcal{A}
and
i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}\backslash\mathcal{A}
, we show that (
A.5
) holds for a.e.
u\in\mathbb{R}
. In particular, if
\widetilde{V}_{i}\equiv\widetilde{V}_{i^{\prime}}
for
i\in\mathcal{A}
and
i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{+}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{j}\backslash\mathcal{A}
, we have

\frac{V_{i}+\mu_{i}+\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}(u-\mu_{j})}{1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}}\equiv\frac{V_{i^{\prime}}+\mu_{i^{\prime}}-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j}\mu_{j}}{1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j}}-u.

This implies that

u=\frac{1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}}{1-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j}}(\mu_{i^{\prime}}-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{i^{\prime}j}\mu_{j})-(\mu_{i}-\sigma_{j}^{-2}\sigma_{ij}\mu_{j}),

which is a single point with a Lebesgue measure of 0 on
\mathbb{R}
. Hence, (
A.5
) holds for a.e.
u\in\mathbb{R}
, which completes the proof.
∎

C.3
Proof of Lemma
A.1

.

For
j,k\in[p]
, we let

\displaystyle\delta_{jk}=\mathbb{I}\{j=k\},\quad\pi^{\mathcal{A}}_{j}(x)=\frac{e^{\beta(x_{j}+v_{j})}}{\sum_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}e^{\beta(x_{j^{\prime}}+v_{j^{\prime}})}},\quad\pi^{\mathcal{B}}_{j}(x)=\frac{e^{\beta(x_{j}+v_{j})}}{\sum_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}e^{\beta(x_{j^{\prime}}+v_{j^{\prime}})}}.

We show by induction that for any
d\geq 1
,
h=(h_{1},\ldots,h_{d})^{\top}\in[p]^{d}
, the partial derivative
\partial_{h_{1}}\ldots\partial_{h_{d}}\varphi^{v}_{s}(x)
is the summation of
K_{d}
terms of the form

\displaystyle\varphi^{(h)}_{k}(x)=

\displaystyle(-1)^{q^{k}}\beta^{d_{1}^{k}}\gamma_{s}^{(d_{2}^{k})}\big(\zeta_{\beta}(x)\big)\prod_{j\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}}\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(h_{j})\pi_{h_{j}}^{\mathcal{A}}(x)\big\}

(C.19)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{B}}}\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}(h_{\ell})\pi_{h_{\ell}}^{\mathcal{B}}(x)\big\}\prod_{r\in\bar{\mathcal{O}}^{k}}\delta_{h_{r}h_{\sigma_{k}(r)}},

where
K_{d}\leq 2^{d}d!
,
k=1,\ldots,K_{d}
,
q^{k}=1,2
,
0\leq d_{1}^{k}\leq d-1
and
1\leq d_{2}^{k}\leq d
are integers such that
d_{1}^{k}+d_{2}^{k}=d
,
\mathcal{O}^{k}\subseteq[d]
is a non-empty subset of
[d]
,
\bar{\mathcal{O}}^{k}=[d]\backslash\mathcal{O}^{k}
,
\mathcal{O}^{k}=\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}\cup\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{B}}
is a partition of
\mathcal{O}^{k}
, and
\sigma_{k}(\cdot)
is a mapping from
\bar{\mathcal{O}}^{k}
to
\mathcal{O}^{k}
.
Then by some algebra, when
d=1
, for any
j\in[p]
, we have that

\displaystyle\partial_{j}\varphi^{v}_{s}(x)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{s}^{\prime}(\zeta_{\beta}(x))\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(j)\pi_{j}^{\mathcal{A}}(x)-\gamma_{s}^{\prime}(\zeta_{\beta}(x))\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}(j)\pi_{j}^{\mathcal{B}}(x),

which is a special case of (
C.19
) with
K_{1}=2
. Now assume that (
C.19
) holds for
d
. Then, for
d+1
and any
h\in[p]^{d+1}
, denote by
h_{1:d}=(h_{j})_{j=1}^{d}
. By some algebra, we have that, for any
j,j^{\prime}\in[p]
,

\displaystyle\partial_{j^{\prime}}\big(\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(j)\pi_{j}^{\mathcal{A}}(x)\big)

\displaystyle=\beta\left\{\delta_{jj^{\prime}}\pi^{\mathcal{A}}_{j}(x)-\pi^{\mathcal{A}}_{j}(x)\pi^{\mathcal{A}}_{j^{\prime}}(x)\right\}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(j)\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(j^{\prime}),

(C.20)

\displaystyle\partial_{j^{\prime}}\big(\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}(j)\pi_{j}^{\mathcal{B}}(x)\big)

\displaystyle=\beta\left\{\delta_{jj^{\prime}}\pi^{\mathcal{B}}_{j}(x)-\pi^{\mathcal{B}}_{j}(x)\pi^{\mathcal{B}}_{j^{\prime}}(x)\right\}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}(j)\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}(j^{\prime}).

(C.21)

Then we have that

\displaystyle\partial_{h_{d+1}}\varphi^{(h_{1:d})}_{k}(x)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{q^{k}}\bigg\{\beta^{d_{1}^{k}}\gamma_{s}^{(d_{2}^{k}+1)}\big(\zeta_{\beta}(x)\big)\!\!\!\prod_{j\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}\cup\{{d+1}\}}\!\!\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(h_{j})\pi_{h_{j}}^{\mathcal{A}}(x)\big\}\!\!\!\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{B}}}\!\!\!\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}(h_{\ell})\pi_{h_{\ell}}^{\mathcal{B}}(x)\big\}\!\prod_{r\in\bar{\mathcal{O}}^{k}}\delta_{h_{r}h_{\sigma_{k}(r)}}

\displaystyle\quad-\beta^{d_{1}^{k}}\gamma_{s}^{(d_{2}^{k}+1)}\big(\zeta_{\beta}(x)\big)\!\!\prod_{j\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}}\!\!\!\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(h_{j})\pi_{h_{j}}^{\mathcal{A}}(x)\big\}\!\!\!\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{B}}\cup\{{d+1}\}}\!\!\!\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}(h_{\ell})\pi_{h_{\ell}}^{\mathcal{B}}(x)\big\}\!\!\!\prod_{r\in\bar{\mathcal{O}}^{k}}\delta_{h_{r}h_{\sigma_{k}(r)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\!\sum_{j\in\mathcal{O}^{k}}\delta_{h_{d+1},h_{j}}\cdot\beta^{d_{1}^{k}+1}\gamma_{s}^{(d_{2}^{k})}\big(\zeta_{\beta}(x)\!\big)\!\!\!\!\prod_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}}\!\!\!\!\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(h_{j^{\prime}})\pi_{h_{j^{\prime}}}^{\mathcal{A}}(x)\big\}\!\!\!\!\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{B}}}\!\!\!\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}(h_{\ell})\pi_{h_{\ell}}^{\mathcal{B}}(x)\big\}\!\!\!\prod_{r\in\bar{\mathcal{O}}^{k}}\!\!\delta_{h_{r}h_{\sigma_{k}(r)}}

\displaystyle\quad-|\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}|\beta^{d_{1}^{k}+1}\gamma_{s}^{(d_{2}^{k})}\big(\zeta_{\beta}(x)\big)\!\!\!\prod_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}\cup\{d+1\}}\!\!\!\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(h_{j^{\prime}})\pi_{h_{j^{\prime}}}^{\mathcal{A}}(x)\big\}\!\!\!\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{B}}}\!\!\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}(h_{\ell})\pi_{h_{\ell}}^{\mathcal{B}}(x)\big\}\prod_{r\in\bar{\mathcal{O}}^{k}}\delta_{h_{r}h_{\sigma_{k}(r)}}

\displaystyle\quad-|\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{B}}|\beta^{d_{1}^{k}+1}\gamma_{s}^{(d_{2}^{k})}\big(\zeta_{\beta}(x)\big)\!\!\!\prod_{j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}}\!\!\!\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(h_{j^{\prime}})\pi_{h_{j^{\prime}}}^{\mathcal{A}}(x)\big\}\!\!\!\!\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{B}}\cup\{d+1\}}\!\!\!\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}(h_{\ell})\pi_{h_{\ell}}^{\mathcal{B}}(x)\big\}\!\!\prod_{r\in\bar{\mathcal{O}}^{k}}\!\!\delta_{h_{r}h_{\sigma_{k}(r)}}\bigg\}.

Hence, we have that (
C.19
) holds for
d+1
, and
K_{d+1}\leq 2(d+1)K_{d}\leq 2^{d+1}(d+1)!
.

Furthermore, for any
d\in[5]
and
h=(h_{1},\ldots,h_{d})^{\top}\in[p]^{d}
, let

U_{h}(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{K_{d}}\beta^{d_{1}^{k}}C_{\gamma}\delta^{-d_{2}^{k}}\!\!\prod_{j\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}}\!\!\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(h_{j})\pi_{h_{j}}^{\mathcal{A}}(x)\big\}\!\!\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{O}^{k}_{\mathcal{B}}}\!\!\big\{\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{B}}(h_{\ell})\pi_{h_{\ell}}^{\mathcal{B}}(x)\big\}\!\!\prod_{r\in\bar{\mathcal{O}}^{k}}\!\!\delta_{h_{r}h_{\sigma_{k}(r)}}.

We have that

|\partial_{h_{1}}\ldots\partial_{h_{d}}\varphi^{v}_{s}(x)|=\left|\sum_{k=1}^{K_{d}}\varphi^{(h)}_{k}(x)\right|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K_{d}}|\varphi^{(h)}_{k}(x)|\leq U_{h}(x).

Meanwhile, we note that for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
with
\max_{j\in[p]}|y_{j}|\leq\beta^{-1}
, we have that

\sum_{j\in\mathcal{A}}\pi^{\mathcal{A}}_{j}(x)=1,\quad\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}}\pi^{\mathcal{B}}_{j}(x)=1,

e^{-2}\pi_{j}^{\mathcal{A}}(x)\leq\pi_{j}^{\mathcal{A}}(x+y)\leq e^{2}\pi_{j}^{\mathcal{A}}(x),\quad e^{-2}\pi_{j}^{\mathcal{B}}(x)\leq\pi_{j}^{\mathcal{B}}(x+y)\leq e^{2}\pi_{j}^{\mathcal{B}}(x).

Then, we have

\sum_{h_{1}=1}^{p}\ldots\sum_{h_{d}=1}^{p}U_{h}(x)\leq K_{d}C_{\gamma}\left(\delta^{-d}\vee(\beta^{1}/\delta^{d-1})\vee\ldots\vee(\beta^{d-1}/\delta)\right)\leq K_{d}^{\prime}\delta^{-d}\log^{d-1}p;

e^{-2d}U_{h}(x)\leq U_{h}(x+y)\leq e^{2d}U_{h}(x),

where
K_{d}^{\prime}=C_{\gamma}K_{d}
. Hence, we have that (
A.16
)-(
A.18
) hold by taking small enough
c_{1}
and large enough
C_{0},C_{1}
, which completes the proof.
∎

C.4
Proof of Claim
A.6
for Lemma
5.2

Proof.

We show (
A.21
) by modifying the rates in equations (76), (77), and (78) of
[
12
]
with our redefined smoothing functions and the anti-concentration condition.
Specifically, we redefine the function
f:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
in Step 1 of the proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
12
]
by

f(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{|I_{d}|}\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi_{s}^{v}\Big(W_{i}^{\sigma}+\frac{tV_{\sigma(i)}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)-\varphi_{s}^{v}\Big(W_{i}^{\sigma}+\frac{tZ_{\sigma(i)}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)\Big],\quad\forall t\in[0,1],

where
\sigma
is a random function with uniform distribution on the set of all one-to-one functions mapping
\{1,\ldots,|I_{d}|\}
to
I_{d}
, and
\sigma
is independent of
V_{1},\ldots,V_{n},Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}
and
\epsilon^{d+1}
;
W_{i}^{\sigma}
is defined as

W_{i}^{\sigma}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}V_{\sigma(j)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j=i+1}^{|I_{d}|}Z_{\sigma(j)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j\notin I_{d}}Z_{j},\quad\forall i=1,\ldots,|I_{d}|.

Then, following the proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
12
]
, to show the validity of (
A.21
), we only need to establish bounds on the derivatives of the function
f(\cdot)
up to the fourth order. Specifically, we prove the following claims to establish the counterparts of the rates in equations (76), (77), and (78) of
[
12
]
.

Claim C.1
.

Under the event
\mathcal{A}_{d}
, we have that

\displaystyle|f^{(2)}(0)|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{5}(pn)}{n^{2}}

(C.22)

\displaystyle+\big(r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}})\big)\left(\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n,1,d}\delta^{-2}\log p}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{3}(pn)}{n}\right).

Proof.

See Appendix
C.4.1
.
∎

Claim C.2
.

Under the event
\mathcal{A}_{d}
, we have that

\displaystyle|f^{(3)}(0)|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{5}(pn)}{n^{2}}

(C.23)

\displaystyle+\big(r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}})\big)\left(\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n,2,d}\delta^{-3}\log^{2}p}{{n}}+\frac{B_{n}^{3}\delta^{-5}\log^{5}(pn)}{n^{3/2}}\right).

Proof.

See Appendix
C.4.2
.
∎

Claim C.3
.

Under the event
\mathcal{A}_{d}
, we have that

|f^{(4)}(\widetilde{t})|\lesssim\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{3}p}{n^{2}}+\big(r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}})\big)\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{3}p}{n},\quad\text{where $\widetilde{t}\in(0,1)$}.

(C.24)

Proof.

See Appendix
C.4.3
.
∎

The rates (
C.22
)-(
C.24
) are obtained by modifying Step 3, Step 4 and Step 5 of the proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
12
]
correspondingly. Combining (
C.22
)-(
C.24
), following the same arguments as in Step 1 of the proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
12
]
, we have that (
A.21
) holds. ∎

C.4.1
Proof of Claim
C.1
via modification of Step 3 in
[
12
]

In Step 3 of the proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
12
]
, to show the new rate with respect to equation (76) in that paper, we first need to show that equation (80) of
[
12
]
still holds for our redefined smoothing functions. Namely, we first show that

\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi_{s}^{v}(x)=\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi_{s}^{v}(x)h^{v}_{s,\delta}(x,0),\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{p}.

(C.25)

Indeed when
h^{v}_{s,\delta}(x,0)=0
, by the property of the function
\zeta_{\beta}^{v}
in (
A.14
), we have that either
\zeta_{\beta}^{v}(x)\leq s
or
\zeta_{\beta}^{v}(x)>s+\delta
, which by the property of the function
\gamma_{s}
indicates that
\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi_{s}^{v}(x)=0
. Hence the claim follows. Then for the error term
\mathcal{I}_{2,1}
defined below equation (79) in
[
12
]
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k\in[p]}\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi^{v}_{s}(W)|]=\!\!\sum_{j,k\in[p]}\!\mathbb{E}[h^{v}_{s,\delta}(W,0)|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\varphi^{v}_{s}(W)|]\leq\!\!\sum_{j,k\in[p]}\!\mathbb{E}[h^{v}_{s,\delta}(W,0)|U_{jk}(W)|]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{0}\delta^{-2}\log p\mathbb{E}\big(h^{v}_{s,\delta}(W,0)\big)\leq C_{0}\delta^{-2}\log p\left(C_{a}(3r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n})+2\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}})\right),

where the last inequality is by Condition
5.4
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big(h^{v}_{s,\delta}(W,0)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big(h^{v}_{s,\delta}(S_{n}^{Z},0)\big)+2\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}})\leq C_{a}(3r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n})+2\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}}).

Hence, replacing the rate in equation (81) of
[
12
]
, we have that

|\mathcal{I}_{2,1}|\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n,1,d}\delta^{-2}\log p}{\sqrt{n}}\left(r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}})\right).

For the error term
\mathcal{I}_{2,2}
defined below equation (79) in
[
12
]
, we define

\chi^{V}_{i}=\mathbb{I}\{\|V_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)\},\quad\chi^{Z}_{i}=\mathbb{I}\{\|Z_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)\},

which correspond to the indicators
\widetilde{V}_{i}
and
\widetilde{Z}_{i}
defined in Step 3 of
[
12
]
. Then when
\chi^{V}_{i}=1
, we have that
h_{s,\delta}^{v}(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma},2C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)/\sqrt{n})=0
. This indicates
h_{s,\delta}^{v}(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma}+\widehat{t}V_{i}/\sqrt{n},0)=0
for any
\widehat{t}\in(0,1)
.
Then following the same arguments as establishing (
C.25
), for all
i\in I_{d}
and
j,k,\ell,r\in[p]
we have that

\displaystyle\chi^{V}_{i}\Big|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\partial_{\ell}\partial_{r}\varphi_{s}^{v}\Big(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{t}V_{i}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)V_{i\ell}V_{ir}\Big|

(C.26)

\displaystyle=\chi^{V}_{i}h_{s,\delta}^{v}\big(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma},2C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)/\sqrt{n}\big)\Big|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\partial_{\ell}\partial_{r}\varphi_{s}^{v}\Big(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{t}V_{i}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)V_{i\ell}V_{ir}\Big|.

Then equation (82) in
[
12
]
also holds in our setting by replacing
h^{y}(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma};x)
with
h_{s,\delta}^{v}\big(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma},2C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)/\sqrt{n}\big)
. Following similar arguments, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle[h_{s,\delta}^{v}\big(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma},2C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)/\sqrt{n}\big)U_{jk\ell r}(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma})]\mathbb{E}[|V_{i\ell}V_{ir}|]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\chi^{V}_{i}\chi^{Z}_{i}h_{s,\delta}^{v}\big(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma},2C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)/\sqrt{n}\big)U_{jk\ell r}(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma})]\mathbb{E}[|V_{i\ell}V_{ir}|]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\chi^{V}_{i}\chi^{Z}_{i}h_{s,\delta}^{v}\big(W,4C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)/\sqrt{n}\big)U_{jk\ell r}(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma})]\mathbb{E}[|V_{i\ell}V_{ir}|]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}[h_{s,\delta}^{v}\big(W,4C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)/\sqrt{n}\big)U_{jk\ell r}(W)]\mathbb{E}[|V_{i\ell}V_{ir}|],

(C.27)

which gives the equivalence of equation (83) of
[
12
]
in our setting. Also by (
A.10
), we have that
C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)/\sqrt{n}\leq\delta
, and again by Condition
5.4
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big(h_{s,\delta}^{v}\big(W,4C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)/\sqrt{n}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big(h^{v}_{s,\delta}(S_{n}^{Z},4C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)/\sqrt{n})\big)+2\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}})

\displaystyle\lesssim r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}}),

and then following the arguments in Step 3 of
[
12
]
, we have

|\mathcal{I}_{2,2}|\lesssim\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{3}p}{n}\big(r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}})\big)+\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{5}(pn)}{n^{2}}.

Hence, (
C.22
) follows.

C.4.2
Proof of Claim
C.2
via modification of Step 4 in
[
12
]

The modification of Step 4 is very similar to that of Step 3. For the error terms
\mathcal{I}_{3,1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{3,2}
defined in Step 4 of
[
12
]
, applying our redefined anti-concentration Condition
5.4
and following the same steps as in Step 3, we have

|\mathcal{I}_{3,1}|\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n,2,d}\delta^{-3}\log^{2}p}{{n}}\left(r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}})\right).

For the error term
\mathcal{I}_{3,2}
, following similar arguments as in showing (
C.26
) and (
C.27
), for any
j,k,\ell,r,h\in[p]
and
i\in I_{d}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\chi_{i}^{V}\left|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\partial_{\ell}\partial_{r}\partial_{h}\varphi_{s}^{v}(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma}+\frac{\widehat{t}V_{i}}{\sqrt{n}})V_{ir}V_{ih}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left(h_{s,\delta}^{v}\big(W,4C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)/\sqrt{n}\big)U_{jk\ell rh}(W)\right)\mathbb{E}(|V_{ir}V_{ih}|),

where
\widehat{t}\in(0,1)
. By Condition
5.4
and following the arguments in Step 4 of
[
12
]
, we have

|\mathcal{I}_{3,2}|\lesssim\frac{B_{n}^{3}\delta^{-5}\log^{5}(pn)}{n^{3/2}}\big(r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}})\big)+\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{5}(pn)}{n^{2}},

which implies that (
C.23
) holds.

C.4.3
Proof of Claim
C.3
via modification of Step 5 in
[
12
]

For the error terms
\mathcal{I}_{4,1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{4,2}
defined at the beginning of Step 5 in
[
12
]
, following the same arguments for showing (
C.26
) and (
C.27
), we have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{d}}\sum_{j,k,\ell,r\in[p]}\mathbb{E}\left(\chi^{V}_{i}\Big|\partial_{j}\partial_{k}\partial_{\ell}\partial_{r}\varphi_{s}^{v}\Big(W_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}^{\sigma}+\frac{\widetilde{t}V_{i}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)V_{ij}V_{ik}V_{i\ell}V_{ir}\Big|\right)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}}\!\!\!\sum_{j,k,\ell,r\in[p]}\!\!\!\mathbb{E}\!\left(h_{s,\delta}^{v}\big(W,4C_{e}B_{n}\log(pn)/\sqrt{n}\big)U_{jk\ell r}(W)\!\right)\!\!\max_{j,k,\ell,r\in[p]}\sum_{i\in I_{d}}\mathbb{E}\left(|V_{ij}V_{ik}V_{i\ell}V_{ir}|\right)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{3}p}{n}\big(r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}})\big).

Following the arguments in Step 5 of
[
12
]
, we have

|\mathcal{I}_{4,1}|\lesssim\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{3}(p)}{n}\big(r_{n}\delta+\varepsilon_{n}+\mathbb{E}(\varrho_{\epsilon^{d+1}})\big)+\frac{B_{n}^{2}\delta^{-4}\log^{3}(p)}{n^{2}}.

The bound for
\mathcal{I}_{4,2}
follows similar arguments, and (
C.24
) holds.

C.5
Proof of Claim
B.1
for Lemma
B.2

.

Recall that
\widetilde{Z}_{j}=-Z_{j}+c_{j}-u
for
j\in[d]
. Given
j\in[d]
, for
i\in[d]\backslash\{j\}
, define the orthogonal residuals of projecting
Z_{i}
and
\widetilde{Z}_{i}
onto
\widetilde{Z}_{j}
as
V_{i}=Z_{i}-\mu_{i}+\rho_{ij}(\widetilde{Z}_{j}+\mu_{j}-c_{j}+u)
and
\widetilde{V}_{i}=-(Z_{i}-\mu_{i})-\rho_{ij}(\widetilde{Z}_{j}+\mu_{j}-c_{j}+u)=-V_{i}
. We have

\displaystyle H_{u,j}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}V_{i}\leq x-\mu_{i}+\rho_{ij}(\widetilde{Z}_{j}+\mu_{j}-c_{j}+u),&\,i\in[d]\backslash j\\
\widetilde{V}_{i}\leq x+\mu_{i}-c_{i}+u-\rho_{ij}(\widetilde{Z}_{j}+\mu_{j}-c_{j}+u),&\,i\in[d]\backslash j\\
c_{j}-u-x\leq x&\end{array}\Bigg|\widetilde{Z}_{j}=x\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}V_{i}\leq x-\mu_{i}+\rho_{ij}(x+\mu_{j}-c_{j}+u),&\,i\in[d]\backslash j\\
-V_{i}\leq x+\mu_{i}-c_{i}+u-\rho_{ij}(x+\mu_{j}-c_{j}+u),&\,i\in[d]\backslash j\\
c_{j}-u-x\leq x&\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}U_{i}\leq x,&\,i\in[d]\backslash j\\
\widetilde{U}_{i}\leq x,&\,i\in[d]\backslash j\\
(c_{j}-u)/2\leq x&\end{array}\right),

where

U_{i}\!=\!(1+\rho_{ij})^{-1}(V_{i}+\mu_{i}-\rho_{ij}(\mu_{j}-c_{j}+u)\!),\,\widetilde{U}_{i}=\!(1-\rho_{ij})^{-1}(\!-V_{i}-\mu_{i}+c_{i}+\rho_{ij}(\mu_{j}-c_{j})\!)-u.

When
x<(c_{j}-u)/2
, we have that
H_{u,j}(x)=g_{u,j}(x)=0
, and (
B.28
) holds.

Next, we show that (
B.29
) holds. When
x>(c_{j}-u)/2
, we have that

H_{u,j}(x)=\mathbb{P}\big(\textstyle\max_{i\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}U_{i}\leq x,\textstyle\max_{i\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\widetilde{U}_{i}\leq x\big).

To apply (
2.10
) of Lemma
2.5
to bound
dH_{u,j}(x)/dx
, we remove the duplicate terms in
\{U_{i},\widetilde{U}_{i}\}_{i\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}
. We first show that for any
i\neq i^{\prime}
,
U_{i}-U_{i^{\prime}}\not\equiv 0
for a.e.
u\in\mathbb{R}
. Indeed, if
U_{i}\equiv U_{i^{\prime}}
, then
(1+\rho_{ij})^{-1}V_{i}\equiv(1+\rho_{i^{\prime}j})^{-1}V_{i^{\prime}}
, and by similar arguments for showing (
C.18
) in Claim
A.5
, we have that
\rho_{ij}\neq\rho_{i^{\prime}j}
, since otherwise we have
Z_{i}-\mu_{i}\equiv Z_{i^{\prime}}-\mu_{i^{\prime}}
, which contradicts our assumption. Then we have that
U_{i}\equiv U_{i^{\prime}}
holds only at

u=\left(\frac{\rho_{ij}}{1+\rho_{ij}}-\frac{\rho_{i^{\prime}j}}{1+\rho_{i^{\prime}j}}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\mu_{i}-\rho_{ij}(\mu_{j}-c_{j})}{1+\rho_{ij}}-\frac{\mu_{i^{\prime}}-\rho_{i^{\prime}j}(\mu_{j}-c_{j})}{1+\rho_{i^{\prime}j}}\right).

Similarly, for any
i,i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}
, if
U_{i}\equiv\widetilde{U}_{i^{\prime}}
, we have that

u=\frac{1+\rho_{ij}}{1-\rho_{i^{\prime}j}}(c_{i^{\prime}}-\mu_{i^{\prime}}+\rho_{i^{\prime}j}(\mu_{j}-c_{j}))-(\mu_{i}-\rho_{ij}(\mu_{j}-c_{j})).

Hence, for a.e.
u\in\mathbb{R}
, the duplicate terms may only be in
\{\widetilde{U}_{i}\}_{i\neq j}
. Denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\subseteq[d]
the subset with duplicate terms in
\{\widetilde{U}_{i}\}_{i\in[d]\backslash j}
removed, i.e., let
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
be any subset of
[d]\backslash\{j\}
such that for any
i,i^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
and
i\neq i^{\prime}
, we have
\widetilde{U}_{i}\not\equiv\widetilde{U}_{i^{\prime}}
. If
[d]\backslash\{j\}\backslash\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\neq\varnothing
, then for any
k\in[d]\backslash\{j\}\backslash\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
, there exists a
k^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
such that
\widetilde{U}_{k}\equiv\widetilde{U}_{k^{\prime}}
. Then, by
x>(c_{j}-u)/2
, applying (
2.10
) of Lemma
2.5
, we have that

\displaystyle dH_{u,j}(x)/dx

\displaystyle=d\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}U_{i}\leq x,&\,i\neq j\\
\widetilde{U}_{i}\leq x,&\,i\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\end{array}\right)/dx=\underbrace{\sum_{i\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}U_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,i^{\prime}\neq i,j\\
\widetilde{U}_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,i^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\end{array}\bigg|U_{i}=x\right)f_{U_{i}}(x)}_{\rm I_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad\underbrace{+\sum_{i\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}}\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}U_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,i^{\prime}\neq j\\
\widetilde{U}_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,i^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\backslash\{i\}\end{array}\bigg|\widetilde{U}_{i}=x\right)f_{\widetilde{U}_{i}}(x)}_{\rm I_{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{\rm(a)}{\geq}\sum_{i\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{ll}U_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,i^{\prime}\neq i,j\\
\widetilde{U}_{i^{\prime}}\leq x,&\,i^{\prime}\neq j\\
(c_{j}-u)/2\leq x&\end{array}\Bigg|U_{i}=x\right)f_{U_{i}}(x)

\displaystyle\overset{\rm(b)}{=}\sum_{i\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}\mathbb{P}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\max_{i^{\prime}\neq i}Z_{i^{\prime}}\leq x\\
\max_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\widetilde{Z}_{j^{\prime}}\leq x\end{array}\bigg|\begin{array}[]{c}Z_{i}=x\\
\widetilde{Z}_{j}=x\end{array}\right)(1+\rho_{ij})\phi_{Z_{i}|\widetilde{Z}_{j}}^{u}(x|x)

\displaystyle\geq(1+\underline{\rho})g_{u,j}(x),

where
f_{U_{i}}(\cdot)
and
f_{\widetilde{U}_{i}}(\cdot)
denote the marginal densities for
U_{i}
and
\widetilde{U}_{i}
respectively; inequality (a) holds as the terms in
{\rm I}_{2}
are positive, and adding back constraints on the duplicate terms in
\{\widetilde{U}_{i^{\prime}}\}_{i^{\prime}\in[d]\backslash\{j\}}
does not change the value of
{\rm I}_{1}
; equality (b) holds by the equivalence that

\{(c_{j}-u)/2\leq x|\widetilde{Z}_{j}=x\}=\{Z_{j}\leq x|\widetilde{Z}_{j}=x\}.

Therefore, (
B.29
) holds for a.e.
u\in\mathbb{R}
, which completes the proof.
∎

C.6
Log-Sobolev inequalities for Gaussian random vectors

.

We provide the Log-Sobolev inequality for Gaussian random vectors
[
3
]
for self-completeness. This lemma is essential in establishing tail probability bounds for Gaussian maximal statistics.
∎

Lemma C.1
.

Let
Z=(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{d})^{\top}
be a standard Gaussian random vector on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and let
F:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be an
L
-Lipschitz function. Then, we have
\mathbb{E}|F(Z)|<\infty
and

\mathbb{P}\big(F(Z)\geq\mathbb{E}(F(Z))+r\big)\leq\exp\big(-r^{2}/(2L^{2})\big),\quad\forall r>0.

Proof.

The inequality holds by Theorem 9 and Theorem 11 in
[
3
]
.
∎

Appendix D
Additional Simulation Results

In this section, we present additional simulation results deferred from Section
4
. Specifically, we explore how the Lévy concentration function
\mathcal{L}(M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon)
scales with
1-\bar{\rho}
, where
\bar{\rho}=\max_{i\in\mathcal{A},j\in\mathcal{B}}\operatorname{Corr}(X_{i},X_{j})
. We consider both full rank and low rank settings. In the full rank setting, we generate
(X_{\mathcal{A}},X_{\mathcal{B}})
by setting
\sigma_{j}^{2}=1
for all
j=1,\ldots,p
and
\text{Cov}(X_{i},X_{j})=\rho
for all
i,j\in[p]
with
i\neq j
. We vary
\rho
across 100 equidistant values between 0.9 and 0.99 to explore how
\mathcal{L}(M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon)
scales with
1-\bar{\rho}=1-\rho
. For the low rank setting, we generate
X_{\mathcal{A}}
and
X_{\mathcal{B}}
by (
4.1
). Here, the entries of
\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
are drawn independently from a standard Gaussian distribution, and the row norms of
\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
are normalized to unity. We repeat the generation of
\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
100 times under the low rank setting to create 100 different values of
\bar{\rho}
and examine the scaling of
\mathcal{L}(M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon)
with respect to
1/\sqrt{1-\bar{\rho}}
and
1/(1-\bar{\rho})
respectively. For all settings, the Lévy concentration function
\mathcal{L}(M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon)
is evaluated at
\varepsilon=0.05
over 500 independent repetitions of the generation scheme.

The results of the full rank setting are shown in Figure
8
(a) and (b). The Lévy concentration function scales linearly with
1/\sqrt{1-\bar{\rho}}
across all settings of
p\in\{2,2000,3000,4000\}
in Figure
8
(a), while a sublinear relationship is observed between the Lévy concentration function and
1/(1-\bar{\rho})
in Figure
8
(b). The results suggest the potential to improve the extra factor of
\sqrt{1-\bar{\rho}}
in the anti-concentration rate of Theorem
2.1
and Theorem
2.4
.

Figure
8
(c) and (d) display the results from the low rank setting. When
p=2
, where
M_{\mathcal{B}}-M_{\mathcal{A}}=X_{2}-X_{1}
reduces to a Gaussian random variable, we observe that
\mathcal{L}(X_{2}-X_{1},\varepsilon)
increases as
1/\sqrt{1-\bar{\rho}}
or
1/(1-\bar{\rho})
increase. For
p\geq 3
, this trend is less pronounced, indicating that there may be room to refine the anti-concentration bounds when the correlations are non-uniform.

\mathcal{L}(\max_{j\in[p]}X_{j},\varepsilon)

(b) Density curves

. (a) Empirical evaluation of the Lévy concentration function scaling linearly with the interval length
\varepsilon
; (b) For the density curves,

is normalized by the sample mean and standard deviation for comparability.

Scaling of the Lévy concentration function at

with

and

respectively. (a) and (b) present the results under the full rank setting, where
\sigma_{j}^{2}=1
for all
j\in[p]
and

for all

with

. The Lévy concentration grows linearly with

. (c) and (d) present the results under the low rank setting. The Lévy concentration increases with

.
